# Lonesome Dove (1985): chapter-by-chapter outline

This outline is for a personal companion app that animates each chapter as the audiobook plays. It is built **from the novel's own text**, from local files `chapters/ch001.txt`–`ch102.txt. Web study guides (GradeSaver, TheBestNotes, Shmoop, LitCharts) were used only to cross-check, and every disagreement is logged in the chapter notes and in `sources.md`. The 1989 miniseries was not used. Events are paraphrased; the only quoted text is proper names, the sign's wording and a five-word opening cue per chapter used for audio alignment.

## Structure

- **102 numbered chapters** (confirmed from the text and the book's table of contents) in **three parts**:
  - **Part I** = ch 1–25. Lonesome Dove, the raid into Mexico, gathering the herd, departure.
  - **Part II** = ch 26–74. Starts with July Johnson in Fort Smith, covers the drive from the Nueces to Kansas, and ends with Jake Spoon's hanging.
  - **Part III** = ch 75–102. Starts with Clara in Nebraska, then Ogallala, Wyoming, Montana, Gus's death, the ranch on the Milk River and Call's journey south.
- Total ≈ 364,000 words.

## Timeline anchors (from the text)

| When | Evidence |
|---|---|
| Story opens in **spring**, very dry year | ch 1 "quiet spring evening"; cattlemen making up spring herds |
| Call aims to be on the trail by **1 April** | ch 12 (three weeks' gathering); ch 19 "nearly April" |
| Herd leaves late March / early April | ch 23–25 |
| Year is after **mid-1876** | Custer's defeat referred to as past (ch 42, 90). Adobe Walls fight (1874) "not two years" earlier (ch 61), so about 1876–77 |
| Wet spring through Texas and the Territory | ch 31–63 (several storms) |
| **Early summer** at the Arkansas; **midsummer** in Kansas | ch 70, 72 |
| Summer in Nebraska / Ogallala | ch 83–88 |
| Late summer → autumn in Wyoming and Montana | ch 89–97 |
| Winter at the Milk River; pigs eaten at Christmas; July proposes in the **first week of January** | ch 98–100 |
| Call leaves the Milk at the **end of May**; reaches Lonesome Dove **late on a day in August** | ch 100–102 |

## Storylines (tag values used in `chapters.json`)

- **Hat Creek outfit**: the drive (Call, Gus, Deets, Pea Eye, Newt, Dish, Jake early on, the O'Briens, the Spettles, the Raineys, Jasper, Soupy, Needle, Bert, Lippy, Bolivar and later Po Campo)
- **Lorena & Jake** / **Lorena (captive)** / **Gus's pursuit**
- **July Johnson**: Fort Smith → Texas → the Canadian → Dodge → Nebraska
- **Elmira**: whiskey boat up the Arkansas → Bent's Fort → hide wagon → Clara's → Ogallala
- **Roscoe**: Fort Smith → Louisa Brooks → Janey → rescued by July → killed on the Canadian
- **Jake & the Suggs**: Fort Worth → Dallas → Doan's Store → Kansas → hanged
- **Clara**: her Nebraska horse ranch
- **Montana ranch** and **Call's return south**

Bought in Mexico, she bites and kicks | ch 1 (named and described); first ridden on-page in ch 7–8 | Wilbarger offers 38 horses for her (ch 12). She throws Call (ch 44). Parker (Wrangler)" and "Deets, Joshua", and adds "We Don't Rent Pigs" and the Latin motto ***Uva uvam vivendo varia fit*** | ch 8 (full history and wording) | Gus pries it off and takes it along (ch 23). Wilbarger ribs Gus about the Latin (ch 9). Gus asks for it over his grave (ch 96). ## Audiobook notes

- The **Lee Horsley** unabridged reading is published by **Phoenix Books / Phoenix Audio**, not Simon & Schuster. Its current digital edition (2016, remastered) runs **36 h 47 m 28 s** (per the OverDrive/Libby listing). It began as a Dove Audio cassette set (24 cassettes in two volumes).
- **Simon & Schuster Audio** released a *different* unabridged edition on 23 Sep 2025 (40th anniversary), read by **Will Patton** with a foreword by **Taylor Sheridan**. It runs about **35 h 49 m**. Audible lists it too.
- I could not find a published track list for either edition, so I can't confirm whether their chapter markers match the novel's 102 chapters. Most modern digital releases mark chapters by the book's numbering, but that is unverified here. To help you check, `chapters.json` includes:
  - `openingCue`: the first five words of each chapter. Match these to the start of each track.
  - `wordCount`, and `estAudioStart_Horsley`: an estimated start time (h:mm) in the Horsley edition, assuming a constant reading pace. Expect drift of several minutes by mid-book.

## Caveats on geography

The novel compresses real distances (for example, "80 waterless miles" from the Platte to Salt Creek, Wyoming, is really far more) and leaves many crossings unnamed. `routes.json` gives best-fit coordinates with a confidence level. **Lonesome Dove, Pickles Gap and the Hacienda Flores are fictional.** Clara's orchard is placed only loosely on the Guadalupe: ch 44 puts it near the San Antonio–Austin route, while ch 96 says "the south Guadalupe."

# Chapters


## Part I


### Chapter 1

*Opening cue:* “When Augustus Came Out on…” · *Words:* 6,037 · *Est. start (Horsley):* 0:00 · *Confidence:* high

- **Storyline:** Hat Creek outfit
- **Where:** Hat Creek Cattle Company headquarters on the edge of Lonesome Dove, Texas (fictional border town on the Rio Grande, south Texas brush country); the springhouse and porch; the wagon yard and horse lots; the ranch house kitchen/dining table; Hat Creek (a dry sandy ditch); the Rio Grande, with Mexico beyond
- **Who (on page):** Augustus (Gus) McCrae, Captain Woodrow F. Call, Pea Eye Parker, Newt Dobbs, Bolivar (the cook), the two blue pigs (sow and shoat)
- **Mentioned:** Deets, Dillard Brawley (one-legged barber), Wesley Pickles (founder of Pickles Gap), the Hell Bitch (Call's mare), Ugly Bobby (old Ranger)
- **When:** Spring (text calls it a quiet spring evening; cattlemen are making up their spring herds). A very dry year. Moon is in the quarter. Era: roughly a decade after the Civil War (late 1870s per most readings; the novel does not give a year here).
- **Herd:** n/a - herd not yet assembled
- **Key events:**
  1. At the end of a hot spring day Gus shoos the blue pigs (who are eating a rattlesnake) off the porch, fetches his whiskey jug from the springhouse and settles in to drink toward sundown.
  1. Call and Pea Eye come up from the lots, then an exhausted Newt; they have hit rock digging the new well, and Call's temper is bad because his mare bit a chunk out of his side.
  1. Bolivar pounds the clapperless dinner bell with a broken crowbar; over sowbelly and beans Gus needles Call with riverboat and whore stories while Call privately judges that the quarter 'rustler's moon' makes it time to raid Mexico for stock.
  1. A squabble over who freed the slaves ends with Gus teasing that Scottish-born Call is no American; Call takes his hat and Henry rifle and walks out into the night.
- **Scenes (visual beats):**
  - *Hat Creek porch and dusty yard* | late afternoon | hot, dry, still; dry year | Augustus, blue pigs: The blue sow and shoat play tug-of-war with a small rattlesnake on the porch until Gus kicks them off.
  - *adobe springhouse* | late afternoon | hot | Augustus: Gus waits in the sun for a coiled rattler to leave, then lifts his burlap-wrapped jug out of the mud and checks it for centipedes before a swig.
  - *porch, rope-bottomed chair* | sunset | sky turning pink over the western flats | Augustus, Call, Pea Eye, Newt: Sweat-blackened Call and Pea Eye, then a mud-caked Newt, trudge up from the lots past the wagons as Gus offers the jug.
  - *yard by the house* | dusk | very still; horses audible swishing tails | Bolivar, all: Bolivar hammers the clapperless dinner bell with a broken crowbar for five minutes while the pigs trot to the house with the men.
  - *ranch house table under a kerosene lamp* | night | moths around the lamp | Augustus, Call, Pea Eye, Newt, Bolivar: Bolivar treats the bite above Call's belt; Gus grabs seconds of beans; the talk sours and Call stands in the doorway with his Henry and leaves.
- **Visual details:** Hat Creek 'offices' = a roofless barn (roof blown off three years earlier) and a couple of patched corrals; wagons in the yard; a little lumpy adobe springhouse; a blacksmith's shop with a forge; a dinner bell with no clapper. · No shade tree within twenty or thirty miles; chaparral flats, dust, horned toads, roadrunners. · The pigs are blue: an old sow and a young shoat; the shoat trails Gus 'quiet as a cat' and eats a lizard. · The Hell Bitch: Call's mare, bought in Mexico; dapple gray; white muzzle; white blaze/streak on the forehead; taller than an Indian pony, shorter-barreled than a thoroughbred; vicious — once kicked Newt into the blacksmith shop. · Call: barely middle-sized but seems big; Gus four inches taller; Pea Eye three inches taller than Gus — tall, lank, awkward-looking, deaf in the left ear, prominent Adam's apple. · Bolivar: old ex-bandit, dirty serape, lard-greasy hair, waxed mustache; drinks brown sugar with a little coffee. · Call's rifle is a Henry; Gus also owns a Henry. Supper: sowbelly and chili beans; goat Gus says tastes fried in tar.
- **Notes:** FIRST APPEARANCE: the blue pigs; Gus's jug in the springhouse; the Hell Bitch (named, described); the clapperless dinner bell and broken crowbar; Call's Henry rifle. Backstory mentions: Stone House fight with the Keechis (Pea Eye's deaf ear) and walking down the Brazos; Prairie Dog Fork of the Red. Pickles Gap is a (fictional) settlement twelve miles off. GradeSaver misspells the barber 'Dillan'; the text has Dillard Brawley.

### Chapter 2

*Opening cue:* “Call Walked The River for…” · *Words:* 4,683 · *Est. start (Horsley):* 0:36 · *Confidence:* high

- **Storyline:** Hat Creek outfit
- **Where:** an old Comanche crossing on the Rio Grande west of Lonesome Dove, near a salt lick (Call sits on a little bluff by a chaparral clump); the Hat Creek porch and yard, Lonesome Dove, Texas; the Dry Bean saloon and the church next door (heard/described, not visited)
- **Who (on page):** Woodrow Call, Augustus McCrae, Pea Eye Parker, Newt Dobbs, Bolivar, the blue pigs
- **Mentioned:** Lorena Wood, Lippy Jones (Dry Bean piano player), Mrs. Pink Higgins (church pianist), Deets, Bolivar's wife and nine daughters
- **When:** Spring, same night as chapter 1 (clear, dry).
- **Herd:** n/a - herd not yet assembled
- **Key events:**
  1. Call walks the river alone for an hour, as he does every night, sitting at the old Comanche crossing; he admits to himself there is no real threat and that he is weary of being 'the Captain' of a small livery business with no troop and no war.
  1. On the porch Bolivar whets his bone-handled knife, trades insults with Gus about wives and daughters, and throws the knife into a wagon side; Newt frets that the two old fighters might someday have a real fight.
  1. Newt privately dwells on his silent love for Lorena Wood, the Dry Bean's whore, whom he has never dared speak to.
  1. Call returns, says it would have been a good night to cross stock; he again puts off Newt's request to ride on a raid, and Gus heads to town to gamble while Call warns him not to let 'that girl' talk him into marriage.
- **Scenes (visual beats):**
  - *little bluff by a chaparral clump at the old Comanche river crossing, west of town* | night | very clear, starry, dry; still | Call: Call sits alone with his Henry, watching Mexico across the Rio Grande and listening to the country.
  - *Hat Creek porch and yard* | night (moonlight) | peaceful and still; Dry Bean piano faintly audible | Bolivar, Augustus, Pea Eye, Newt, pigs: Bolivar's thin blade flashes in the moonlight on the whetstone; he flings the knife and it quivers in a wagon twenty feet away while Gus twirls his Colt's cylinder.
  - *porch step* | night | still | Newt, the blue shoat: Newt, hunched on the bottom step, scratches the friendly shoat's ears and daydreams about what he will say to Lorena; Pea Eye snores against the railing.
  - *porch* | late night | still | Call, Augustus, Newt: Call returns with his rifle in the crook of his arm and lights a smoke; Newt is sent to bed; Gus steps over the sleeping shoat and walks off toward the saloon.
- **Visual details:** Bolivar's knife: fine bone-handled knife, handle of mule-deer horn, thin blade; he spits on the whetstone. · Pea Eye's 'Sunday knife': a Bowie with a fourteen-inch blade in a big sheath. · Gus: Colt revolver in a holster hung on the chair back; an old silver pocket watch, pencil stub and notebook kept in the old black vest he always wears (he times Bolivar urinating). · The Dry Bean's piano has wheels; deacons built a ramp and a board track so it can be pushed next door to the church on Sundays; once it derailed and lost two legs and was played in the street. · Lorena lives upstairs over the Dry Bean and uses its back stairs. · Bolivar once gave Newt a serape to use as a blanket; Newt sleeps in a bunk above Bolivar's. · Lippy Jones plays the piano and has an unhealed bullet hole in his belly.
- **Notes:** Backstory: Comanches now 'all but whipped'; Fort Phantom Hill mentioned as site of an old fight. Gus claims a year of college in Virginia. Gus calls Newt 'son' while looking at Call (early hint of Newt's paternity). Newt has had jaundice in the past. The Comanche crossing near a salt lick is fictional and not locatable; place it on the Rio Grande just upstream (west) of the town.

### Chapter 3

*Opening cue:* “Lorena Had Never Lived in…” · *Words:* 3,638 · *Est. start (Horsley):* 1:05 · *Confidence:* high

- **Storyline:** Lorena & Jake (Lorena's backstory)
- **Where:** Lorena's upstairs room over the Dry Bean saloon, Lonesome Dove, Texas (window looks over brown flats to the Rio Grande and Mexico); (memory) Mobile, Alabama; (memory) Vicksburg, Mississippi and Baton Rouge, Louisiana; (memory) Mosby Marlin's house near Gladewater, east Texas; (memory) a hotel in San Antonio, Texas; (memory) the stage road toward Matamoros, ending in Lonesome Dove
- **Who (on page):** Lorena Wood, Augustus McCrae (in her recollection of a July visit)
- **Mentioned:** Mosby Marlin, Mosby's mother and two sisters, John Tinkersley, Xavier Wanz, Lippy Jones, Lorena's parents
- **When:** Present frame not dated (implicitly the same spring); the $10 conversation is a memory from a previous July.
- **Herd:** n/a - herd not yet assembled
- **Key events:**
  1. Lorena, nearly twenty-four and stuck in hot Lonesome Dove, fixes on San Francisco as the cool place she means to reach; Gus's long talk about the city is part of why.
  1. Her history: orphaned at seventeen in Baton Rouge, taken by Mosby Marlin to a windowless, mosquito-plagued house near Gladewater and prostituted to pay his debts; rescued/bought by the handsome gambler John Tinkersley, who took her to a San Antonio hotel, beat her and pimped her.
  1. On the way to the border she tried to shoot Tinkersley with an uncocked gun, clubbed his jaw instead, and he bit her upper lip (her small scar); he abandoned her penniless in Lonesome Dove, calling her murderous, and she cooked for Xavier at the Dry Bean until Gus became her first customer.
  1. (Memory) One blistering July day Gus paid her ten dollars in gold simply to hear her life story; she declined, returned the coin, and they went down to play cards.
- **Scenes (visual beats):**
  - *Lorena's room above the Dry Bean* | midday | blazing; land looks white in the heat | Lorena: Lorena gazes out her window over flat brown land and gray chaparral toward the river and Mexico, dreaming of cool San Francisco.
  - *(memory) drafty unglazed house near Gladewater* | night | smoke from smoke pots, clouds of mosquitoes | Lorena, Mosby Marlin: (memory) Lorena lies choking in smoke-pot haze, going silent; later she slashes Mosby's face with his own quirt and is locked in the cellar.
  - *(memory) a room on the road to the border* | unspecified | unspecified | Lorena, John Tinkersley: (memory) Lorena jams Tinkersley's pistol into his stomach; it won't fire, so she smashes his jaw with it while he bites her lip.
  - *(memory) Lorena's room* | afternoon | blistering July heat; sprinkled bedsheets drying instantly | Lorena, Augustus: (memory) Gus sits with a foot on his knee twirling his spur rowel and tosses her a ten-dollar gold piece to buy conversation.
- **Visual details:** Lorena: nearly 24, blonde (hair colour is established elsewhere; here: 'developed well', very pretty), faint small scar just above her upper lip from Tinkersley's bite; habitually silent. · Gus: a mass of white hair (turned white at thirty); always wears spurs, even off horseback; rides a big black horse 'that could pull three stagecoaches'; paid her five dollars in gold per visit. · Tinkersley: tallest, prettiest, cleanest man she'd seen; had his fingernails trimmed; middling gambler. · Lorena liked riding a horse with a sidesaddle (Tinkersley sold it). · From her window, early mornings, she has seen Call, Gus and riders trailing stock through the low brush east of town.
- **Notes:** Mostly backstory; few present-tense scenes, so animation will lean on memories. Lorena has been in Lonesome Dove 'several months' per ch. 2. GradeSaver and BestNotes agree on the content. Real-world anchors for her past: Mobile AL, Vicksburg MS, Baton Rouge LA, Gladewater TX, San Antonio TX.

### Chapter 4

*Opening cue:* “When Augustus Left Call sitting…” · *Words:* 4,106 · *Est. start (Horsley):* 1:27 · *Confidence:* high

- **Storyline:** Hat Creek outfit
- **Where:** the street of Lonesome Dove, Texas, from the Hat Creek wagon yard across the sandy bed of Hat Creek; the Pumphreys' store (lamp lit); the Dry Bean saloon (bar, rough whittled tables, piano, back door, outside back stairs to Lorena's room); Hat Creek porch
- **Who (on page):** Augustus McCrae, Dish (Dishwater) Boggett, Lorena Wood, Lippy Jones, Xavier Wanz, an armadillo, the blue pigs
- **Mentioned:** Clara Allen, Gus's two dead wives, Therese (Xavier's late wife), Shanghai Pierce, the Pumphreys and their pregnant widowed daughter
- **When:** Spring (Dish says he leaves 'next week' to trail cattle north; trail-driving season beginning). Same night as chapters 1–2.
- **Herd:** n/a - herd not yet assembled
- **Key events:**
  1. Gus strolls into town under a clear starry sky, straps on his heavy Colt dragoon on the dry creek bed, and is startled by an armadillo.
  1. At the Dry Bean, Xavier (fussy, melancholy Frenchman) snatches Lippy's filthy bowler hat and flings it out the back door; Dish Boggett, broke after losing his wages in Matamoros, has ridden up the river hoping Lorena will give him credit.
  1. Gus reflects that Lorena reminds him of Clara Allen, the woman he loved most; he gets up a card game with Dish, Lippy, Xavier and Lorena, and helps Lorena win so she brightens up.
  1. Outside, Dish says he's signed with Shanghai Pierce's UU outfit; Gus gives him the two dollars he'd asked for and invites him to sleep on the Hat Creek porch; Dish slips up Lorena's back stairs and Gus walks home to find the pigs asleep on the porch.
- **Scenes (visual beats):**
  - *Lonesome Dove street and the dry bed of Hat Creek* | night | dry, very clear; starlight casts shadows off every bush | Augustus, armadillo: Gus jumps sideways as a rolling ball of shadow — an armadillo — scuttles across his path; a lamp glows at the Pumphreys' store.
  - *inside the Dry Bean* | night | n/a indoors | Xavier, Lippy, Dish, Lorena, Augustus: Lippy pounds 'My Bonnie' on the piano while Xavier, in white shirt and limp shoestring tie, wipes tables and then flings Lippy's bowler out the back door.
  - *card table in the Dry Bean* | late night | rustler's (quarter) moon crossing the sky | Augustus, Dish, Lorena, Xavier, Lippy: Lorena, winning a pot, laughs and punches Gus's arm while love-struck Dish watches.
  - *street outside the Dry Bean, under Lorena's lit window* | night | moonlight | Augustus, Dish: Gus presses two dollars on Dish; Dish rehitches his rangy sorrel and hurries up the back stairs; two shadows show in the yellow window.
  - *Hat Creek porch* | night | still | Augustus, pigs: The two blue pigs sleep snout to snout on the porch; Gus lets them be and goes in the back door.
- **Visual details:** Gus's sidearm: an old Colt dragoon with a seven-inch barrel. · Dish Boggett: barely 22, walrus mustache the colour of a prairie dog (between yellow and brown); rides a rangy sorrel. · Lorena: fine head of soft blond hair, slightly hollow cheeks, side-looking cloudy eyes, very pale skin; wears cheap powder. · Xavier Wanz: small Frenchman born in New Orleans; year-round white shirt, trimmed little mustache, a black shoestring as a bow tie; face quivers like a trapped rabbit when angry; brings out a linen tablecloth once a year. · Lippy: stringy strips of gray hair; huge lower lip (like a saddlebag flap) that flutters as he mouth-breathes; brown bowler hat (made in Philadelphia) he also uses as ashtray and pillow. · Dry Bean tables: rough, splintery, edges whittled lopsided by cowboys; no tablecloths. · Town has a church beside the saloon; the Pumphreys run a store.
- **Notes:** Clara Allen introduced by name here (Gus's great love, married a horse trader). Gus's two wives died (pleurisy; scarlet fever). Dish had delivered horses at Matamoros and ridden ~100 miles upriver. Shanghai Pierce is a real Texas cattleman (UU/'Shanghai' Pierce). BestNotes wrongly says Gus refused Dish the loan outright; the text shows he gives it after the game.

### Chapter 5

*Opening cue:* “Whatever Subject Augustus had on…” · *Words:* 3,672 · *Est. start (Horsley):* 1:52 · *Confidence:* high

- **Storyline:** Hat Creek outfit
- **Where:** Hat Creek backyard (Dutch oven over mesquite coals, upturned lard kettle); Hat Creek kitchen and back porch, Lonesome Dove, Texas; the stage road running north through the brush country toward San Antonio (seen from the back porch)
- **Who (on page):** Augustus McCrae, Woodrow Call, Newt Dobbs, Pea Eye Parker, Bolivar, Dish Boggett, the blue pigs, Deets and Jake Spoon (seen approaching at the very end)
- **Mentioned:** Lorena Wood, Shanghai Pierce, Jack Jennell (a bad shot, anecdote)
- **When:** Spring; the morning after chapters 1–4. The new well has been six weeks in the digging.
- **Herd:** n/a - herd not yet assembled (Call first floats the idea of a drive here)
- **Key events:**
  1. Gus rises about 4 a.m., bakes sourdough biscuits in a Dutch oven outside, and reads the Prophets (Amos) on an upturned lard kettle as the sun rises over dewy chaparral; the pigs and a stiff, half-awake Call join him.
  1. Breakfast is chaos: Newt asleep upright with knife and fork, Bolivar sick and spilling coffee grounds into the eggs, Pea Eye and Dish relieving themselves off the back steps; Dish is dazed with love after his night with Lorena.
  1. Call, who has been mulling a trail drive for a year, offers Dish work and announces they are raiding into Mexico that night and may make up a herd; Gus scoffs, Dish agrees to wait a day or two.
  1. From the back porch Call spots two riders far up the San Antonio stage road; Gus recognises Deets on his white gelding and, by his peculiar slump in the saddle, Jake Spoon.
- **Scenes (visual beats):**
  - *Hat Creek backyard* | pre-dawn to sunrise (~4-6 a.m.) | dew on the chaparral; red sky in the east, then clear bluish air | Augustus, blue pigs, goats in the brush: Gus sits on the big black upturned kettle with his Bible while biscuits brown in the Dutch oven; the rising sun turns dew-wet chaparral into glittering diamonds and rainbows.
  - *backyard* | sunrise | dew | Augustus, Call, pigs: Call steps out pulling on his shirt; the dew-damp blue pigs stand in front of Gus as if hoping to eat the Bible; Dish sleeps on the porch with his hat over his eyes and his head on his saddle.
  - *kitchen table* | early morning | unspecified | Newt, Augustus, Call, Bolivar, Pea Eye, Dish, blue shoat: Newt dozes sitting bolt upright with knife and fork; Bolivar spills coffee grounds into the frying eggs; the shoat peers in the doorway.
  - *back porch looking north up the stage road* | morning | heat waves shimmering on the road | Call, Dish, Newt, Augustus: Two distant riders waver in the heat haze on the straight road; Gus, licking honey off his finger, names them: Deets and Jake Spoon.
- **Visual details:** Gus's sourdough pot has been going over ten years; biscuits eaten with honey. · A huge black lard kettle, upturned, serves as Gus's morning seat. · Newt has a cowlick; Pea Eye's pants have about fifteen small buttons. · A three-gallon syrup can serves as the grease bucket. · Deets: black man, wears old quilted pants (his knees show through); rides a big white gelding called Wishbone. · Jake Spoon rides a pacing bay and habitually slumps toward his horse's off side. · The stage road runs straight north through brush before reaching the first gully.
- **Notes:** FIRST APPEARANCE: Deets's horse Wishbone (white gelding); Jake's pacing bay (Jake himself arrives in ch. 6). Deets had been sent to San Antonio three days earlier with a bank deposit. Dish's alternative was a sure job back on the Matagorda (Texas coast). The Hat Creek operation leases just enough land to hold small lots of stock. GradeSaver and BestNotes both place Jake's arrival here — consistent.

### Chapter 6

*Opening cue:* “The Name Struck Newt like…” · *Words:* 5,497 · *Est. start (Horsley):* 2:14 · *Confidence:* high

- **Storyline:** Hat Creek outfit
- **Where:** Hat Creek wagon yard, back porch and kitchen, Lonesome Dove, Texas; the old cistern; (told) Fort Smith, Arkansas — a plank saloon on a wide street by the river; (told) Montana, past the Yellowstone near the Milk River; (mentioned) Pickles Gap, on the road Jake and Deets rode
- **Who (on page):** Jake Spoon, Deets, Augustus McCrae, Woodrow Call, Newt Dobbs, Pea Eye Parker, Dish Boggett, Bolivar, a pig on the back porch
- **Mentioned:** Maggie (Newt's late mother), July Johnson (Fort Smith sheriff), the dead dentist (sheriff's brother, and the mayor), Clara Allen, Tom Bynum, Xavier and Lippy, 'Mr. Dobbs', Jesus the bootmaker
- **When:** Spring, same morning as chapter 5. Jake's killing was 'not three weeks' earlier. Maggie has been dead nine years; Jake left ten years ago.
- **Herd:** n/a - herd not yet assembled
- **Key events:**
  1. Newt is overwhelmed: Jake Spoon was his mother Maggie's kindest visitor, gave him his first ride and first boots, and Newt privately suspects Jake is his father; Jake left ten years ago and Maggie died about a year later.
  1. Jake and a beaming Deets ride into the yard; Jake's pacing bay is ridden down to the bone, which tells Call and Gus he is running from trouble; Call pointedly sets Dish to well-digging to test whether he'll keep his word.
  1. Over fried meat Jake praises Montana — grass, water, buffalo above the Yellowstone — and urges them to beat everyone there with free Texas cattle.
  1. Jake admits he accidentally killed a dentist in Fort Smith, Arkansas, less than three weeks earlier: firing a borrowed buffalo gun at a drunk mule skinner, the bullet went through a plank wall and killed the dentist across the street — who was the mayor and brother of sheriff July Johnson.
  1. Gus drops the remark that one of them (Jake or Call) is probably Newt's father; Call takes his rifle and leaves.
- **Scenes (visual beats):**
  - *Hat Creek wagon yard* | morning | dusty, sky whitening with heat | Jake, Deets, Newt, Call, Augustus, Pea Eye, Dish: Jake and a grinning Deets ride up to the back porch kicking up puffs of dust; Jake, in brown hat and vest with his pearl-handled pistol, asks what's for breakfast.
  - *yard by the barn* | morning | hot | Deets: Deets, in patched quilt pants with cotton ticking poking out and a battered cavalry cap, leads the gaunt horses to the roofless barn.
  - *back porch / kitchen* | morning | hot | Jake, Augustus: Jake, face red from scrubbing with a filthy sacking towel, complains about the dirt and the pig on the porch.
  - *kitchen table* | mid-morning | hot | Jake, Call, Augustus, Deets, Bolivar (asleep on his stool): Jake daintily picks at his food and a whittled match while telling of Montana and the dead dentist; Call stands in the doorway fidgeting.
- **Visual details:** Jake Spoon: coffee-coloured eyes, small mustache, curly black hair with a sizable bald spot on top, brown vest, brown hat, pearl-handled pistol; fastidious eater; rides a pacing bay (here gaunt). · Deets: big white teeth; quilt-patchwork pants snagged by thorns; old cavalry cap found in the 1850s. · Newt keeps a small bundle of his mother's things: beads, combs, a scrapbook, magazine cut-outs, a scratchy picture of his Alabama grandfather. · Fort Smith (per Jake): a pretty river town with wide streets; catfish.
- **Notes:** FIRST APPEARANCE: Jake Spoon (on-page); his pearl-handled pistol. July Johnson named for the first time. Jake mentions Cheyenne, Sioux, Blackfeet and the Milk River — Montana setup. Backstory: Pea Eye once shot a wolf and killed Call's horse 'little Billy' in the Wichita country. Jake's reputation as a pistol shot rests on one lucky hip shot. GradeSaver compresses this chapter and ch. 7 slightly differently (it puts Clara-in-Nebraska in ch. 7, which matches the text).

### Chapter 7

*Opening cue:* “Jake Spoon Stood in the…” · *Words:* 4,852 · *Est. start (Horsley):* 2:47 · *Confidence:* high

- **Storyline:** Hat Creek outfit
- **Where:** Hat Creek house doorway and kitchen, Lonesome Dove, Texas; shade of the adobe springhouse; scrubby pasture behind the house (Bolivar squats in the chaparral); the new well with its windlass, by the corrals; the horse lot with a snubbing post; (told) Ogallala, Nebraska; the North Platte; (memory) Austin, Texas — Clara's parents' store; the Brazos; Fort Phantom Hill
- **Who (on page):** Jake Spoon, Augustus McCrae, Bolivar, Dish Boggett, Woodrow Call, the Hell Bitch, the blue pigs
- **Mentioned:** Clara Allen, Bob Allen, Maggie, Deets (his old lousy sheepskin coat), Black Beaver (Delaware scout), Kit Carson, Captain King, Shanghai Pierce, Harwell (lost his toes to a horse)
- **When:** Spring, same day as chapters 5–6 (late morning).
- **Herd:** n/a - herd not yet assembled
- **Key events:**
  1. Jake and Gus reminisce; Jake reports he glimpsed Clara outside a store in Ogallala — she and horse-trader Bob Allen live on the North Platte in Nebraska, have two girls, and lost their boys.
  1. (Memory) Gus recalls Clara at sixteen: her parents were killed in the great Indian raid of 1856 and she ran their Austin store; she refused both him and Jake and married dull Bob Allen from Kentucky.
  1. Gus and Jake drink from the jug in the springhouse shade until Jake falls asleep with his hat over his face; Gus decides not to tell Jake about Lorena, sure he'll find her.
  1. Gus teases a sweat-soaked Dish at the well windlass, then watches Call snub, blanket and saddle the Hell Bitch, tying up a hind foot so she stands on three legs.
  1. Call abruptly proposes they go north to Montana; Gus mocks it as a wilderness, but warms to it on realising the route crosses the Platte where Clara lives; Call says Newt could run the ranch.
- **Scenes (visual beats):**
  - *doorway of the low Hat Creek house* | morning | hot; sky already white | Jake, Augustus, Bolivar: Jake watches Call and Deets walk to the barn; Bolivar wakes, grumbles, and walks off into the chaparral carrying a rawhide lariat.
  - *shaded side of the adobe springhouse* | late morning | heat waves rising off flint-hard pasture | Jake, Augustus, blue pigs: The two men squat with backs to the cool adobe passing the jug; Jake spots Bolivar's bare backside under a chaparral bush, then naps with his hat over his face.
  - *new well by the corrals* | late morning | hot | Dish, Augustus: Dish, soaked through to his hatband and belt, cranks the windlass hauling buckets of dirt while Gus grins at him.
  - *horse lot, snubbing post* | late morning | hot | Call, the Hell Bitch, Augustus: The mare kicks the saddle twenty feet; Call ropes her near hind foot to the post so she stands on three legs and accepts the saddle, ears pricked at the watching men.
  - *lot fence* | late morning | hot | Call, Augustus: Call, smoking at the fence, gazes north across the dusty flats and proposes driving cattle to Montana.
- **Visual details:** Deets once owned a sheepskin coat as heavy as a sheep for fifteen years; Gus burned it for lice. · Springhouse: adobe, cool on the unsunned side. · Well: a windlass and big dirt bucket. · Hell Bitch: watches with one eye, trembles with indignation, ears pointed at the men. · Clara at sixteen: breathtakingly pretty, spirited.
- **Notes:** Key setup: the idea of the drive to Montana is born here. History given: Rangers kept on the border during the Civil War at the Governor's request, which brought Call and Gus to Lonesome Dove; they've been there nearly ten years; money banked in San Antonio. Call has never been north of the Arkansas River. Gus jokes about meeting on the Powder River (foreshadowing). BestNotes says Gus goes to visit Lorena in this chapter — in the text he only considers it and goes to watch Call instead. Clara's family store was in Austin (relevant later for 'Clara's orchard').

### Chapter 8

*Opening cue:* “By The Middle of the…” · *Words:* 6,608 · *Est. start (Horsley):* 3:17 · *Confidence:* high

- **Storyline:** Hat Creek outfit
- **Where:** the unfinished well and the shaded alleyway of the Hat Creek barn, Lonesome Dove, Texas; the Hat Creek sign — an old root-cellar door nailed to a corner of the corrals facing the road into town; behind the Dry Bean saloon, under Lorena's corner room; the town street (Pumphreys' store; a Mexican saddlemaker behind the general store); the bank of the Rio Grande; inside the Dry Bean
- **Who (on page):** Newt Dobbs, Pea Eye Parker, Dish Boggett, Deets, Woodrow Call, the Hell Bitch, Wilbarger, Chick (Wilbarger's hand), Lorena Wood, Jake Spoon, Xavier Wanz, Lippy Jones, a coyote
- **Mentioned:** Augustus (author of the sign), Juan Cortinas (Mexican raider, rides a gray)
- **When:** Spring, same day as chapters 5–7 (afternoon).
- **Herd:** n/a - herd not yet assembled. (Wilbarger's own herd is gathered beyond the Nueces, bound for Kansas.)
- **Key events:**
  1. In blistering afternoon heat Call rides the lathered mare back from a twenty-mile lope and tells the well-diggers to rest: they are going to Mexico tonight. Newt hopes he's included.
  1. Two riders stop to read Gus's elaborate sign (history told: names added for Pea Eye and for Deets as 'Deets, Joshua', rent/sale lines, 'We Don't Rent Pigs', and a Latin motto Gus can't translate).
  1. The riders are Wilbarger, a trail boss whose remuda was stolen by Mexicans and who needs forty horses for a herd waiting beyond the Nueces bound for Kansas, and his pockmarked hand Chick, who asks for the yellow-haired whore; Dish claims she's his sister.
  1. Dish walks to the Dry Bean and hears Lorena's cornshuck bed creaking with another man; he stalks the town ready to kill, sits by the river an hour, then returns to find her flushed and sharing a glass with Jake Spoon — Jake cheerfully buys him drinks and Dish gets drunk singing at Lippy's piano.
- **Scenes (visual beats):**
  - *down in / beside the new well* | mid-afternoon | scorching; no breeze | Newt, Pea Eye, Dish, Call on the Hell Bitch: Call rides the lathered gray mare right up to the well and orders the sweating men to knock off and rest.
  - *barn alleyway (a little shade)* | afternoon | hot | Deets, Dish, Newt, Pea Eye: Deets patches his quilt pants with a big needle from a cigar box; Dish and Newt play root-the-peg and Newt digs the peg out with his teeth, dirt up his nose.
  - *the sign on the corral corner, then the barn* | afternoon | hot | Wilbarger, Chick, Newt, Pea Eye, Dish, Deets: A stocky red-faced man on a fine black horse and a tiny pock-faced man on a swaybacked grulla stop to read the crowded sign, then trot over asking for forty horses.
  - *behind the two-storey frame Dry Bean, under Lorena's window* | afternoon | still and hot | Dish: Half-tucking his shirt, Dish freezes at the creak of Lorena's cornshuck bed overhead, then checks his pistol loads.
  - *Rio Grande bank, then inside the Dry Bean* | late afternoon | hot, bright | Dish, a coyote, Lorena, Jake, Xavier, Lippy: Dish watches a coyote eat a frog in the brown shallows; back at the saloon he sees flushed Lorena sipping from Jake's glass, and is waved in to drink.
- **Visual details:** THE SIGN (painted on an old plank root-cellar door on the corral corner facing the road): firm name 'Hat Creek Cattle Company and Livery Emporium'; proprietors Capt. Augustus McCrae and Captain W. F. Call; 'P. E. Parker (Wrangler)'; 'Deets, Joshua'; rent line (horses and rigs); sale line (cattle and horses); no goats or donkeys; 'We Don't Rent Pigs'; and the Latin motto 'Uva uvam vivendo varia fit'. Deets sits in its afternoon shade. · The one rentable rig is a spring buggy with no springs, bought from Xavier after it crushed his wife. · Deets: woolly hair just turning gray; big black hands. · Pea Eye's foot is the size and shape of a scoop shovel. · Wilbarger: stocky, red-faced, about Call's age; rides a black horse. Chick: tiny, feisty, deeply pockmarked, big pistol on his leg, rides a nearly swaybacked grulla (mouse-gray); cackling laugh. · Dry Bean: two storeys of frame lumber, swinging doors, hitch rail; Lorena's corner room has a bad bed with a cornshuck mattress. · Jake with hat pushed back shows a strip of sun-untouched white forehead. · Big boys load rolls of barbed wire into a wagon at the Pumphreys' store.
- **Notes:** FIRST APPEARANCE: the Hat Creek sign and its Latin motto (full contents given in this chapter); Wilbarger and Chick. Gus is two years older than Call. The motto is Gus's misremembered version of a Latin proverb (usually 'uva uvam videndo varia fit', 'a grape changes colour by seeing another grape'); Gus doesn't know its meaning. Sabinas (Mexico) is mentioned as Dish's joke destination. Juan Cortinas is a real historical figure (Juan Cortina). Study guides agree.

### Chapter 9

*Opening cue:* “Augustus Was on the front…” · *Words:* 4,325 · *Est. start (Horsley):* 3:57 · *Confidence:* high

- **Storyline:** Hat Creek outfit
- **Where:** Hat Creek front porch, Lonesome Dove, Texas; the sandy bed of Hat Creek; the horse lots, barn and old stone stock tank; the Rio Grande crossing at dusk, heading southeast into Coahuila, Mexico, toward the Hacienda Flores (~30 miles away)
- **Who (on page):** Augustus McCrae, Wilbarger, Chick, Woodrow Call, Jake Spoon, Dish Boggett, Newt Dobbs, Deets, Pea Eye Parker, Bolivar, the blue pigs, the Hell Bitch
- **Mentioned:** Pedro Flores, Shanghai Pierce, Lorena
- **When:** Spring, same day as chapters 5–8 (late afternoon to dusk).
- **Herd:** n/a - herd not yet assembled
- **Key events:**
  1. Wilbarger rides his black horse right up to the porch (the shoat walks under it); he needs forty broke horses for 3,000 cattle and eleven men; Call promises a hundred horses by sunup at $35 each, only three available today; Wilbarger's brand is HIC on the left hip and he says he studied Latin at Yale.
  1. Call reveals the plan to Gus: raid the Hacienda Flores, largest ranch in Coahuila, sell Wilbarger forty horses and keep the rest as a remuda for a drive north with hired cowboys.
  1. Dish, blind drunk after two bottles with Jake, vomits on hands and knees in Hat Creek; Jake fires three shots over the house to stop Bolivar's bell; Call suspects Gus and Jake set Dish up and lets him ride.
  1. At the lots Call hands Newt his own oiled pistol and gun belt — Newt feels grown for the first time; at sundown the seven-man outfit crosses the river and rides southeast for the Hacienda Flores.
- **Scenes (visual beats):**
  - *Hat Creek front porch* | late afternoon | hot | Augustus, Wilbarger, Chick, Call, blue pigs: Wilbarger trots his big black horse to the porch; the startled shoat walks right under it; Call comes out shirttail loose, having made a cactus-pulp poultice for his bite.
  - *sandy bottom of Hat Creek* | late afternoon | hot | Dish, Newt, Augustus, Jake, Call: Dish retches on hands and knees in the dry creek while Gus and Jake chat on the bank; he crawls up the low bank, stands, trips on a mesquite root and falls flat.
  - *creek bank facing the house* | late afternoon | hot | Jake, Bolivar (offscreen): As Bolivar clangs the bell early, Jake draws his pearl-handled pistol and fires three shots high over the house; the clanging never stops.
  - *barn and horse lot, stone stock tank* | sunset | clear; bullbats dipping over the tank | Call, Newt, Deets: Call hands Newt a holstered, freshly oiled Colt and belt; Newt buckles it on and ropes his dun gelding Mouse on the first throw while Deets grins.
  - *Rio Grande crossing* | dusk / afterglow | soft empty sky | Call, Augustus, Jake, Deets, Pea Eye, Dish, Newt: Seven riders splash across the river and ride southeast into Mexico toward the Hacienda Flores.
- **Visual details:** Wilbarger's big black horse is well broke; he loosens its girth when dismounting. Chick rides a gray (grulla). · Wilbarger's horse brand: HIC on the left hip; his horses are shod. · Gus reads an old brass pocket watch (ch. 2 called it silver). · Newt's pistol: a Colt with a wooden grip and cool blue barrel; holster smells of saddle soap. · Deets carries an old Walker Colt 'the size of a ham', worn only on trips. · Gus's horse for the raid: a big solid sorrel called Mud Pie. Newt's favourite: a dun gelding called Mouse. · An old stone stock tank built by Deets and Call stands in the lots.
- **Notes:** FIRST APPEARANCE: Mouse (Newt's dun gelding); Mud Pie (Gus's sorrel); Deets's Walker Colt; Newt's first gun. Note Gus's horse: ch. 3 (Lorena's memory) mentions Gus on a big black horse; here Deets saddles Mud Pie, a sorrel — Gus evidently rides more than one horse. Gus's watch described as silver in ch. 2 and brass here. Hacienda Flores is fictional; Coahuila is the Mexican state across from this stretch of the Rio Grande (roughly ~30 miles southeast of the fictional town). The crew of seven: Call, Gus, Jake, Deets, Pea Eye, Dish, Newt.

### Chapter 10

*Opening cue:* “The First Difference Newt noticed…” · *Words:* 4,613 · *Est. start (Horsley):* 4:23 · *Confidence:* high

- **Storyline:** Hat Creek outfit
- **Where:** Coahuila, Mexico: dark empty brush and gully country southeast of the Rio Grande; a ridge where a herd of wild longhorns is flushed; a burnt-out wranglers' out camp of the Hacienda Flores (roofless adobe lean-to, broken rail corral), a few miles south of the main 'big camp'
- **Who (on page):** Woodrow Call, Augustus McCrae, Jake Spoon, Deets, Pea Eye Parker, Dish Boggett, Newt Dobbs, Allen O'Brien, Sean O'Brien, a thin hobbled mule and a small donkey
- **Mentioned:** Pedro Flores, Lorena, Lippy
- **When:** Spring; the night following chapter 9.
- **Herd:** n/a - herd not yet assembled
- **Key events:**
  1. The seven ride southeast at a long trot for two hours by thin moonlight through empty brush, flushing a herd of perhaps 400 longhorns that runs west; Call plans to collect them the next night.
  1. Deets, who judges distances uncannily, reckons them five miles from a Flores out camp; Call splits the party — Gus, Jake, Pea Eye and Dish go for the horse herd, while Call, Deets and Newt go to capture the wranglers at another out camp.
  1. Deets hears singing: at a burnt-out camp two Irishmen, Allen O'Brien (bald, older) and his young brother Sean, are drinking the last of a bottle and arguing whether to eat their mule or donkey.
  1. Call steps into the firelight; Sean yells 'murderers', leaps on the hobbled mule and is pitched off, to Deets's delight. The brothers were aiming for Galveston; Call warns that Pedro Flores will hang them and promises to send two horses back for them.
- **Scenes (visual beats):**
  - *brush country south of the river* | night | thin moon; bright stars; Milky Way like a speckled cloud | the seven riders: Riders top a ridge at a lope and spook a big herd of longhorns that thunders away west; the men dismount and relieve themselves beside their horses.
  - *trail toward the out camps* | night | clear, still | Newt, Dish, Deets, Pea Eye: Newt rides in the middle at Mouse's easy trot, trying to memorise landmarks and feeling ever more lost; Dish glares at Jake's back hoping a stray bullet finds him.
  - *low ridge above the burnt-out camp* | night | moonlight; white woodsmoke rising | Call, Deets, Newt: The three creep to a broken rail corral; smoke drifts from a roofless hut and Irish voices sing a song Lippy plays.
  - *inside the broken adobe lean-to* | night | firelight | Allen O'Brien, Sean O'Brien, Call, Deets, Newt: Call appears in the firelight; Sean bolts onto the hobbled mule, which crashes down and throws him back toward the fire while Deets roars with laughter.
- **Visual details:** Mouse has an easy trot and a hard lope. · The out camp: a lean-to hut with piles of adobe bricks, walls full of holes, roof burnt off; rails of the old corral scattered. · Allen O'Brien: short, stout, nearly bald, dirty shirt, jolly and courteous. Sean O'Brien: short, stout, very young (about Newt's age), fine baritone voice, broke a tooth on an unsoaked bean. · Their animals: a thin mule (hobbled) and a small donkey; a sack of dried beans; an empty bottle. · Call carries his rifle in the crook of his arm; horses tied to a stunted tree.
- **Notes:** FIRST APPEARANCE: Allen and Sean O'Brien. The O'Briens had meant to reach Galveston. Newt identifies their song by a title (no lyrics reproduced). Exact geography is invented; place the camps ~25-30 miles into Coahuila southeast of the fictional Lonesome Dove. Study guides agree.

### Chapter 11

*Opening cue:* “Augustus Soon Found the horse…” · *Words:* 4,882 · *Est. start (Horsley):* 4:51 · *Confidence:* high

- **Storyline:** Hat Creek outfit
- **Where:** a valley south of the old line camp, Coahuila, Mexico (Wilbarger's stolen horses); a narrow valley several miles north, about a mile from the Hacienda Flores headquarters (main Flores horse herd); a long chaparral plain just south of the Rio Grande (dense thicket of chaparral, prickly pear and mesquite); the old Comanche crossing of the Rio Grande, about a mile above Lonesome Dove
- **Who (on page):** Augustus McCrae, Jake Spoon, Woodrow Call, Deets, Newt Dobbs, Pea Eye Parker, Dish Boggett, the Hell Bitch, Mouse, Sardine (Pea Eye's horse), four Mexican horse thieves (unnamed vaqueros)
- **Mentioned:** Allen and Sean O'Brien, Pedro Flores, Wilbarger, Lorena
- **When:** Spring; the night after chapter 10 into the following dawn.
- **Herd:** n/a - herd not yet assembled (horse remuda of 100+ acquired here)
- **Key events:**
  1. Gus's party finds only about forty horses, and they turn out to carry Wilbarger's HIC brand — his stolen remuda; Newt ropes two for the Irishmen and Gus rides off leading them.
  1. Call splits the party three ways: Deets (quietly given responsibility), Dish and Jake drive Wilbarger's horses home; Gus fetches the O'Briens; Call, Pea Eye and Newt go after the main Flores herd.
  1. They find over a hundred horses in a narrow valley a mile from the hacienda and push them northwest past it; Newt, riding left point alone, broods on hanging and on how crossing the river turns crime into a game.
  1. Near the river the herd collides in the dark with a second herd being run south out of Texas by four Mexican horse thieves; Newt is swept along in the dust-choked stampede, hears Call's Henry fire, and at sunrise finds Call waiting at the Comanche crossing with well over a hundred horses — Newt weeps secretly with relief and only says good morning.
- **Scenes (visual beats):**
  - *valley south of the old line camp* | night | moonlit | Augustus, Jake, Pea Eye, Dish, Call, Deets, Newt: Newt ropes a horse from a gentle bunch that trots up to sniff Mouse; Gus laughs on seeing the HIC brands on the left hips.
  - *narrow valley near the Hacienda Flores* | night (about four hours before sunup) | thin moon hanging in the west | Call, Pea Eye, Newt: Three riders quietly circle a herd of a hundred-plus horses and start it northwest, waving ropes and saying nothing.
  - *chaparral plain south of the river, by a dense thicket* | pre-dawn | dark; choking white dust | Newt, Mouse, Mexican horse thieves, Call, Pea Eye: Two horse herds slam together around the thicket; Newt, low over Mouse, is carried in the torrent as Mouse leaps a chaparral bush and a strange rider cursing in Spanish runs alongside.
  - *rise above the Rio Grande* | dawn | red line on the eastern horizon; fading moon over a silver river | Newt: Newt sees the river curve at the old Comanche crossing, realises he is home, and smears tears through the dust on his cheeks.
  - *the Comanche crossing* | sunrise | first sunlight streaming through dust over brown water | Call on the Hell Bitch, Newt, Pea Eye on Sardine, the horse herd: Call waits on the bank, Henry in the crook of his arm, the lathered gray mare slinging her head; the horses pour into the river to drink and roll in the shallows while Pea wades Sardine in stirrup-deep.
- **Visual details:** Wilbarger's horses: gentle, run to a frazzle, HIC on the left hip. · Newt makes rope hackamores for the Irishmen's horses; Jake complains of his borrowed 'old trotter'. · Call's Henry: said to be the only Henry on the border — everyone else carries lighter Winchesters. · The Hell Bitch at dawn: lathered, head up, keen ears, surefooted 'as a cat', far from used up; she tries to bite other horses. · Pea Eye's mount: a big rangy bay called Sardine. · Newt has only seen snow in pictures; once saw thin ice on the porch water bucket.
- **Notes:** FIRST APPEARANCE: Sardine (Pea Eye's rangy bay). Minor inconsistency in the novel: in ch. 1 Gus offers to lend Newt 'my Henry', but here Call is called the only man on the border with a Henry. BestNotes and GradeSaver both place the split and the collision here; GradeSaver stops before the stampede. The crossing 'a mile above Lonesome Dove' is the same old Comanche crossing Call visits in ch. 2.

### Chapter 12

*Opening cue:* “If Wilbarger Was Impressed at…” · *Words:* 5,877 · *Est. start (Horsley):* 5:21 · *Confidence:* high

- **Storyline:** Hat Creek outfit
- **Where:** Hat Creek corrals and the 'opry seat' (top rail), Lonesome Dove, Texas; Hat Creek back porch, yard and kitchen; (memory) the widow Mary Cole's backyard clothesline in Lonesome Dove; (memory) the Dry Bean, Maggie's room; Call heads northeast toward the brush country (to the Raineys'); (mentioned) Sabinas, Mexico; Vera Cruz; Pickles Gap
- **Who (on page):** Wilbarger, Chick, Deets, Dish Boggett, Woodrow Call, Pea Eye Parker, Newt Dobbs, Augustus McCrae, Allen O'Brien, Sean O'Brien, Bolivar, the Hell Bitch, Mouse, Sardine
- **Mentioned:** Jake Spoon (gone to the Dry Bean), Mary Cole (widow), Josh Cole (her husband, killed by lightning), Maggie, Maude Rainey (née Grove)
- **When:** Late winter/early spring — Call reckons three weeks' work will put them on the trail by the first of April, so this is about early-to-mid March.
- **Herd:** n/a - herd not yet assembled (100+ horse remuda held near town; to be moved upriver)
- **Key events:**
  1. At the corrals Wilbarger cuts out his 38 HIC horses and offers all 38 for Call's gray mare; Call refuses. Wilbarger tries to hire Dish, warns that Montana is too far, cold and full of bears, and rides off; Jake has already gone straight to the Dry Bean.
  1. Teased about marrying the tall widow Mary Cole, Pea Eye recalls helping her rescue her wash in a sudden thunderstorm, and — separately — once overhearing Maggie in her room calling the Captain by his first name before Newt was born (the book's strongest early hint that Call is Newt's father).
  1. Gus rides in with the two Irishmen perched helplessly on big silver-studded Mexican saddles taken, with ten skinny horses, from drunk bandits in Sabinas; Sean weeps at the treeless, grassless yard, having expected snow and green America.
  1. Call plans to be on the trail by the first of April, tells Gus to move the horse herd upriver so Pedro can't retake it, and rides his sorrel Sunup northeast to hire hands, starting with the Raineys.
- **Scenes (visual beats):**
  - *Hat Creek corrals* | early morning | dusty | Wilbarger, Deets, Chick, Dish, Pea Eye on the top rail, Newt, Call on the Hell Bitch: Dish works the gate letting HIC horses through; unsaddled Mouse and Sardine lie down and roll in the dust; Wilbarger eyes the mare and offers 38-for-1.
  - *corral gate* | morning | unspecified | Chick, Wilbarger: Chick leans to shut the gate, his hat falls off and nobody picks it up; he must climb down, red-faced, while Wilbarger waits.
  - *(memory) Mary Cole's backyard* | afternoon | sudden thunderstorm, wind, tumbleweeds, then sun and rainbows | Pea Eye, Mary Cole: (memory) Tall Mary wrestles snapping sheets on the line in pouring rain; Pea, water streaming off his hat brim, stands clutching her bloomers.
  - *Hat Creek back yard* | morning, after breakfast | hot, bare | Augustus, Allen, Sean, Dish, Newt, Call: The short-legged Irishmen sit stranded on oversized silver-studded Mexican saddles; Sean dangles from the horn as his tired horse half-bucks, and even Call laughs.
  - *yard and brush* | late morning | hot | Dish, Newt, Deets, Call: Dish teaches Newt to rope low bushes; Call saddles his sorrel Sunup and rides northeast into the brush country.

GradeSaver's page skips chapters 12-15 entirely.

Retelling the afternoon of chapter 8 from Lorena's side: Jake walks into the Dry Bean relaxed and friendly, guesses she belongs in San Francisco, and within an hour she drops her silence and tells him her whole story; he confides that he killed a dentist and may not live to help her, which makes her want to protect him.

  - *Dry Bean* | late afternoon / night | unspecified | Lorena, Lippy, Dish, Jasper Fant: Lorena laughs aloud at drunk Dish singing, then later stares a skinny cowboy, Jasper Fant, into backing away without a word.

  - *against the water trough inside the corral* | night, moonrise | clear; thin white hook of a moon | Deets, horses: Deets sits hidden in ground shadow, rifle across his lap, watching horses suck water and gazing up at the crescent moon, imagining a ladder to it.

- **Mentioned:** Elmira Johnson, Joe Boot, Benny (Ben) Johnson, the dead mayor and dentist, Jake Spoon, Dee Boot, old man Darton, Sylvester Johnson, Shanghai Pierce's cowboys, the two famous Texas Rangers (Call and McCrae, unnamed)
- **When:** not stated. Benny was killed six weeks earlier; July and Elmira have been married four months. Streets dry lately.
- **Herd:** n/a - July Johnson storyline (herd not present)
- **Key events:**
  1. Sheriff July Johnson, 24 and still yellow from jaundice caught at a trial in Missouri, sits outside the Fort Smith jail with his 48-year-old deputy Roscoe Brown, reflecting on his hard year and new marriage.
  1. Peach Johnson, his huge widowed sister-in-law, crosses the street carrying a red rooster and demands to know when he will go after Jake Spoon for killing her husband Benny, the mayor and dentist.
  1. When the rooster pecks her, Peach wrings its neck on the spot and tosses the head into the weeds.
  1. Roscoe recalls the muddled aftermath of the accidental shooting; July, feeling responsible for the town rather than for his unkind brother, resolves he must pursue Jake, likely toward San Antonio.
  1. July walks home at milking time, rejecting Roscoe's request for Joe to come play dominoes.

  - *Main street of Fort Smith* | afternoon | dry | Peach Johnson, July Johnson, Roscoe Brown: Nearly six-foot Peach picks her way across the street holding a flapping red rooster.
  - *In front of the jailhouse porch* | afternoon | dry | Peach Johnson, July Johnson, Roscoe Brown: Pecked, Peach wrings the rooster's neck; the body flops jerking in the dust and she holds it out by the feet till it stills.

- **Visual details:** Jail with a single cell; its lock is broken so a chain is wrapped around the bars · July: 24, not tall but sturdy, still visibly yellow with jaundice; once lifted an anvil as a boy · Roscoe: 48, always whittling sticks away to nothing; claims a bad back · Peach: very large, nearly six feet tall, loud; carries a red rooster, wrings its neck without getting blood on herself · Headless rooster pumping blood into the street dust; head tossed into weeds by the jailhouse porch · Elmira (described): short, skinny, brunette; Joe Boot (described): 12, skinny, with big, slightly bulging eyes · Joe practises roping stumps and the milk-pen calf with a found rope
- **Notes:** Opens Part II. FIRST APPEARANCE: July Johnson, Roscoe Brown, Peach Johnson (Elmira and Joe described but offstage). Fort Smith is on the Arkansas River (river mentioned as where Roscoe and Joe fish for catfish). Places mentioned: Missouri (the trial), San Antonio, Mexico, Kansas (Elmira said to be from there). Jake's shooting of Benny is recounted as largely accidental; half the saloon first blamed a mule skinner. Rivermen and passing cowboys (Shanghai Pierce's men) are the town's main troublemakers. - **Who (on page):** Roscoe Brown, Peach Johnson, Charlie Barnes, old Sabin the ferryman, Renfro the barkeep, old lady Harkness, townspeople

- **Key events:**

  1. Roscoe searches the empty cabin, finds mouse-nibbled cornbread and no shoes, and suspects a bear, but Peach insists Elmira ran off.
  1. Old Sabin the ferryman confirms a woman boarded a whiskey traders' boat heading upriver toward Bent's Fort; the whole town learns within half an hour.
  1. Harangued by old lady Harkness and caught asleep after emptying his whiskey bottle, Roscoe is ordered by Peach to ride to Texas and find July.
  1. Next morning, full of foreboding, Roscoe saddles his tall white gelding Memphis before a watching crowd and heads for Texas.

  - *July's cabin* | morning | dew on the weeds | Roscoe Brown, Peach Johnson, Charlie Barnes: They find the loft holding only a couple of quilts and cornbread on the stove gnawed by mice; Roscoe soaks his trousers in dewy weeds looking for tracks.
  - *Ferry landing on the Arkansas* | morning | unspecified | Roscoe Brown, old Sabin the ferryman: Sabin shrugs that he saw a woman board the whiskey boat and took her for a whore.
  - *Inside the jail* | afternoon | still heat | Roscoe Brown, Peach Johnson, Charlie Barnes: Roscoe wakes sweating on his couch beside an empty bottle to find Peach and Charlie staring down at him again.
  - *In front of the jail* | morning | beautiful, hint of summer | Roscoe Brown, Peach Johnson, Charlie Barnes, townspeople: Roscoe mounts tall white Memphis, who kicks dust onto Charlie's shiny shoes, and takes a last look at the river before riding for Texas.
- **Visual details:** Charlie Barnes: short, fat banker and deacon, the only man in town who wears a necktie daily, shiny shoes · Empty cabin: slab of cornbread on the cookstove nibbled by mice; loft with a pallet of two quilts · Weeds around the cabin wet with dew · A farmer with a mule team in the otherwise empty street · Old lady Harkness: former Mississippi schoolteacher, helps in her son's general store · Roscoe's whiskey bottle kept under his couch 'for toothache' · Memphis: big, tall white gelding, big-footed, slow to start but steady; Roscoe's mount for ten years, named for his hometown · Roscoe packs a bedroll and ties on a rifle scabbard
- **Notes:** FIRST APPEARANCE: Memphis (Roscoe's white gelding), Charlie Barnes, old Sabin, old lady Harkness. Places named: Catfish Grove (nearest town, 14 miles), Bent's Fort (whiskey boat's destination upriver), Little Rock (Roscoe's only prior trip), Memphis (Roscoe's origin), Mississippi, Texas, San Antonio, California and Canada (Peach's gibes). Roscoe rides white horses because he heard Indians fear them. The fort itself is said to be half a mile away. GradeSaver says Peach tells Roscoe to go after Elmira; in the text old lady Harkness first suggests it, and Peach then orders him to go find July.

*Opening cue:* “The First Good Wash Lorena…” · *Words:* 2,010 · *Est. start (Horsley):* 10:41 · *Confidence:* high

- **Storyline:** Lorena & Jake
- **Where:** Shady spot on the south bank of the Nueces River, south Texas, amid mesquite and prickly pear brush country; North bank of the Nueces, under a big mesquite, where Jake and Lorena re-camp
- **Who (on page):** Lorena Wood, Jake Spoon, Deets
- **Mentioned:** Captain Call, Augustus McCrae, Xavier Wanz
- **When:** not stated; hot. Early in the drive: Lorena reflects it is 'only the second day' of their journey (late March/early April by earlier chapters).
- **Herd:** Several miles south of the Nueces River, pushing through the brush country; Deets scouting the crossing
- **Key events:**
  1. After a hard day in mesquite thickets, ahead of the herd's dust, Lorena halts at the Nueces; Jake is drunk and feverish, his thorn-stabbed thumb gone purple.
  1. Lorena bathes chest-deep in the cold green river; a tub-sized snapping turtle on the bank startles her and Jake misses it four times shooting left-handed.
  1. Jake, too sick to manage, collapses on her on the grass; Lorena props him against a tree and starts coffee.
  1. Deets swims his horse across, digs the thorn tip out of Jake's thumb with a heated sewing needle, and warns of a storm and rising river, urging them to cross now.
  1. Deets leads Lorena's balking mare across as it swims; on the far bank he tells her to cook early, douse the fire, and not tie horses to big trees, then rides back to guide Call to the crossing.
- **Scenes (visual beats):**
  - *South bank of the Nueces, under a shade tree* | afternoon | hot, cloudless, white sky | Lorena Wood, Jake Spoon: Wobbly, drunk Jake slumps against a tree with his bottle while Lorena ties the horses and makes camp.
  - *In the Nueces* | afternoon | hot | Lorena Wood, Jake Spoon: Lorena stands chest-deep washing off the dust; a huge snapping turtle sits at her entry point and Jake's pistol shots plop into the mud as it slides away.
  - *Riverbank campsite* | afternoon | clear | Lorena Wood, Jake Spoon, Deets: Deets rides his horse swimming across from the far side, dismounts dripping, and probes Jake's swollen thumb with a heated needle, holding up the tiny yellow thorn tip with a grin.
  - *Crossing the Nueces* | sunset | clear, sun dropping | Lorena Wood, Deets, Jake Spoon: Lorena clenches the mare's mane with eyes shut as Deets leads the swimming horse across, water to her waist; Jake waits unconcerned on the far bank.
  - *North bank under a big mesquite* | sunset | clear, storm expected from the south | Lorena Wood, Deets, Jake Spoon: Deets glances at the southern sky, gives his storm warnings, and rides into the Nueces for the third time.
- **Visual details:** The Nueces: green water, cold beneath the surface; shady bank with grass and no mesquite · Snapping turtle as big as a tub · Jake's thumb turned from white to purple; he shoots left-handed and misses four times · White sky overhead; the herd's dust visible far behind to the south from clearings · Deets keeps a large needle in a little packet in his saddlebag for sewing his pants · Lorena's brown mare balks, whirls, shows the whites of her eyes, then swims well · Bedrolls tied high on the horses for the crossing · Jake throws his bedroll under a big mesquite on the far bank
- **Notes:** Handle the sexual content discreetly. The Nueces is the first named river of the drive. Deets predicts sand, then lightning, around sundown (realised in ch 31-32). GradeSaver says Deets extracts 'the needle' from Jake's hand - it is a thorn, removed with a needle. Lorena's 'second day' seems compressed relative to the herd's timeline; recorded as stated.

  - *Point of the herd* | dusk | sand beginning to blow; heavy half-light | Newt, Dish Boggett, Soupy Jones: Slouching Dish grins at the news; Soupy ties his bandana over his nose as snaking streams of sand slide under the cattle's bellies like running wolves.

  - *Flooded brush country* | night | sheets of rain, then drizzle | Newt: Water streams off Newt's hat brim front and back; a wet coyote darts past, and Mouse slides into a gully, then plods on through hock-deep water.

  - *Under a low cutbank of the Nueces* | night | lightning so bright every wavelet shows; rain | Jake, Lorena: The two shiver under a mud-caked tarp at the water's edge while the hobbled horses jump and fight their hobbles in the flashes.

- **Notes:** The river is not named in this chapter; identified as the Nueces from ch30 (Lorena's first wash was in the Nueces) and ch34-35 (Gus swims the same muddy river back to the herd, which then crosses the Nueces).   - *Deck near the casks* | dawn | misty | Fowler, traders, the dead trader, Elmira: Men strip the red-haired dead man of boots, belt and pockets and heave him over; his body bumps the boat and drifts into the mist.

The edition also misprints 'Port Smith' once for Fort Smith. Exact stretch of the Arkansas is not named; the text says the river winds out of the trees onto the plains. Elmira has been in Kansas before (a rescued Indian captive was pointed out to her there). Joe is twelve. Shmoop says Big Zwey threw the man overboard; the text says Fowler and a couple of men did it after Zwey killed him. TheBestNotes says two whiskey traders fought; text says a trader and a buffalo hunter.

- **Storyline:** Roscoe
- **Where:** Pine woods along the Army trail between Fort Smith, Arkansas and Texas; A small boggy creek; Roscoe's first camp about ten miles from Fort Smith; Louisa Brooks's stump-filled field, cabin and rail corral in the Arkansas woods near the trail, west of Fort Smith
- **Who (on page):** Roscoe Brown, Memphis (Roscoe's horse), Louisa Brooks, a pack of wild pigs led by a brown boar, Louisa's mule team, Louisa's chickens, Ed, a big rattlesnake
- **Mentioned:** July Johnson, Elmira, Joe, Peach Johnson, Charlie Barnes, Jake Spoon, Benny Johnson, Jim (Louisa's late husband), Betsie (Roscoe's long-dead sweetheart)
- **When:** Not stated; hot and sultry nights suggest late spring/early summer. Roscoe left Fort Smith only the day before meeting Louisa.
- **Herd:** not stated in chapter
- **Key events:**
  1. Hours out of Fort Smith, wild pigs stampede Memphis; later the horse bogs in a creek and Roscoe loses a boot in the mud and sleeps sitting up in terror.
  1. Following the Army trail, Roscoe finds a big woman in men's clothes, Louisa Brooks, pulling stumps with mules; she puts him to work and a lashing root knocks him flat.
  1. Over a supper of nothing but corn bread she proposes marriage; he refuses and beds down behind the cabin, fearing her pet rattler Ed.
  1. At sunup Louisa straddles and seduces the half-asleep Roscoe in the weeds while chickens watch.
  1. Torn, Roscoe keeps his duty to July, says he may stop back, and rides west with mixed feelings as Louisa hitches her mules to another stump.
- **Scenes (visual beats):**
  - *Open pine woods near Fort Smith* | morning | unspecified | Roscoe, Memphis, wild pigs: A swift brown boar leads a pig pack after the fleeing white horse as Roscoe empties his pistol and drops his bullets.
  - *A small creek* | afternoon | unspecified | Roscoe, Memphis: Memphis bogs to the knees; Roscoe whips him out with a lariat and loses a boot deep in the mud.
  - *Louisa's stump field* | sunset | hot | Roscoe, Louisa, mules: Roscoe hacks roots with an ax as the mules strain; a freed root whips out like a snake and knocks him on his back.
  - *Louisa's cabin* | night | hot and still | Roscoe, Louisa: By a small coal-oil lamp with bugs circling, Louisa eats corn bread from the pan and proposes marriage.
  - *Behind the cabin in wet grass* | dawn | sultry, humid, mist; sun breaks through | Roscoe, Louisa, chickens, Ed: Barefoot Louisa, feet wet with dew, sits astride the startled Roscoe as a few chickens look on and Ed crawls over a nearby log.
- **Visual details:** Memphis: a white, tallish horse, lazy, afraid of pigs · Louisa: large, fair-looking, brown hair, men's clothes and floppy hat, shirt sweated through; later a dress, men's brogans on bare feet, sunbonnet · Forty or fifty pulled stumps lined up at the south edge of the field · Large clean roomy cabin, rail corral, well water, chickens scratching; no pigs, no milk cow · Ed: a big rattlesnake that lives behind the cabin · Roscoe: 48, skinny, mustache, sleeps in long johns; bedroll tarp flops on Memphis's flank · Louisa's husband Jim buried right behind the house
- **Notes:** Roscoe rides west on the Army trail from Fort Smith toward Texas. Louisa is from Alabama, has buried three husbands and had eight children, and owns a section of land. The sexual scene should be handled discreetly in visuals. GradeSaver typo 'mills' for mules. Shmoop says he slept in the barn; text says outside against the back wall of the cabin. TheBestNotes says spring water; text says well water. FIRST APPEARANCE: Louisa Brooks and Ed the rattler.

- **Notes:** July names the river 'the Red' on sight, meaning they are about to reach Texas. Sedgwick says he left about a thousand bugs in Little Rock. FIRST APPEARANCE: Sedgwick (only appearance per file search). July's jaundice is referenced in other chapters, not here. Shmoop wrongly calls Joe the sulky silent one; it is July. TheBestNotes says Joe was unnerved by Sedgwick's insight; the text gives that reaction to July.

  - *San Antonio River crossing* | morning | clouds breaking to the west | Jasper, Soupy, Newt, Augustus: Jasper gallops his horse into the river and it stumbles; Soupy laughs.

- **Notes:** The men speculate how far north moccasins live, naming the Cimarron, Arkansas and Platte rivers. Pea Eye's recurring dream of a big Indian begins to trouble him again after leaving Lonesome Dove. Call voices the purpose of the drive: get the land in Montana before settlers arrive. TheBestNotes says Allen 'weeps silently' and that the crossing was narrow; text says shallow. GradeSaver says Allen 'ceases speaking altogether' (text: rides in silence).

  1. The rotten wagon bed has burst and the supplies float; Bol walks up with the pigs and resigns, secretly because he fired his ten-gauge while dreaming and spooked the mules.
  1. Call gives Bol a gentle gelding; Bol rides south bareback on a serape, regretting his choice but planning to beat the old dinner bell in Lonesome Dove, while Newt grieves another loss.

  - *In the creek* | afternoon | hot | Augustus, Newt, Rainey boys, Pea Eye, Lippy: Gus on Malaria ropes a spinning wheel to tip the wagon while the boys lift Lippy's mud-covered head from the water.

- **Notes:** The creek is unnamed. Bol has cooked for them ten years and has never been more than two days' ride from the border. The dinner bell and crowbar recur here (not first appearance). - **Storyline:** Roscoe

- **Who (on page):** Roscoe Brown, Memphis, four drunk soldiers from Missouri, old Sam, Janey
- **Mentioned:** July Johnson, Pete Peters (Fort Smith barber), Bill (who traded Janey), a Dutchman Janey lived with, Janey's dead family and her brother taken by Indians
- **When:** Not stated; hot days and heavy dew. Some days to weeks after Roscoe left Fort Smith.
- **Herd:** not stated in chapter
- **Key events:**
  1. Lost among settlers who can't direct him, Roscoe joins four drunk soldiers heading to Buffalo Springs, gets violently sick in their springless wagon, and is heaved onto Memphis at the Red River.
  1. The soldiers point him southwest toward San Antonio; at dusk he stops at a cabin where a hostile old man, Sam, skins a possum and claims the girl inside as bought property.
  1. Sam drinks Roscoe's whiskey and gives him no supper; in the night Roscoe hears him beat and force the girl but does not intervene.
  1. Next day a wasp nest drops into Roscoe's lap; the girl, Janey, who has followed on foot, packs his stings with mud, kills a rabbit and a bullfrog and cooks them.
  1. Janey says she crippled Sam's knees with a pan; Roscoe lets her ride double for a day or two to guide him toward San Antonio.

  - *Banks of the Red River* | afternoon | heat shimmer | Roscoe, soldiers, Memphis: Too weak to mount, Roscoe slides to the ground clutching a stirrup until the laughing soldiers toss him onto Memphis like a sack of potatoes.
  - *Old Sam's cabin clearing* | dusk | moon rises bright later | old Sam, Janey, Roscoe: A tobacco-stained old man on a stump skins a possum while a thin barefoot girl in a cotton-sack dress appears in the dark doorway.
  - *Wooded trail* | morning | heavy dew | Roscoe, Memphis, Janey: A wasp nest falls in Roscoe's lap; after loping clear he strips off his shirt and spots the girl ducking behind a bush.
  - *Shaded creek bank* | midday | unspecified | Janey, Roscoe, turtles: Janey smacks a leaping bullfrog with a stick, fries rabbit and frog legs, and gray turtles gather to nibble scraps in the creek.
- **Visual details:** Four soldiers, two on horseback and two in a springless mule wagon · Old Sam: tobacco-stained beard, hard-looking; paid twenty-eight skunk hides for the girl · Janey: thin, fourteen or fifteen, pretty, dirty brown hair, barefoot, scratched legs, bruised arms (blue and yellow), torn dress made from a cotton sack, low husky voice, very fast runner · Crude cabin with wide cracks between logs, no light inside · Roscoe's face swells; one eye swollen shut from a sting; mud poultices on his face · Frog legs keep jumping in the pan · Janey rides behind Roscoe, toes hooked in the girth, holding the saddle strings
- **Notes:** FIRST APPEARANCE: Janey. The text does not say whether Sam's cabin is north or south of the Red; Roscoe calls Sam the first person he saw in Texas, so it is south of the river. Buffalo Springs is named as the soldiers' Texas destination. Janey came from San Antonio and was being taken to Fort Worth by Bill until traded to Sam 'up there by Waco' (the geography is loose; Waco is well south of the Red). 'The Territory' (Indian Territory) lies west. The chapter contains period racial language; do not reproduce. GradeSaver says Roscoe lets Janey come to San Antonio; the text says only for a day or two while he decides.

  - *Night camp* | night | unspecified | Augustus, Allen, Lippy, Bert, Jasper, Pea Eye: Gus plays cards with the Irishman and Lippy for wages he's already won while the men wonder why the Captain rides off alone.
  - *Breakfast fire* | morning | fine day | Augustus, Dish, Call, Lippy, Jasper: Gus pours beaten eggs into a big skillet as Dish protests, and Call grumbles the coffee would float a stove lid.
  - *Hill above Clara's orchard* | morning | fine | Augustus, Call, Malaria, Hell Bitch: Gus sits on Malaria looking down at a pool below live oaks, tears on his cheeks and mustache ends, then wipes them with a bandana.

- **Notes:** Hell Bitch is not a first appearance (named in ch1). Gus tells Call he knows Newt is Call's son, saying Maggie told him; Call denies it. Gus recalls proposing to Clara after his second wife died. Memory references to the high plains and the Pecos country (Gus's eyesight). Call rides east to Austin; Gus swings north to the grove. TheBestNotes says Gus first sees the camp; text: Call spots it, Gus identifies Lorena. TheBestNotes calls Hell Bitch a filly; text: mare.

- **Storyline:** Lorena & Jake
- **Where:** Jake and Lorena's camp by a cold pool at the foot of a limestone bluff, rolling plain to the west, west of Austin, Texas

  1. A big Indian on a bay stallion with a silver-trimmed saddle rides in, waters, and names himself Blue Duck; he says he'd been told to kill both Rangers, trades threats with Gus, and rides off.
  1. Gus explains Blue Duck is a Comanchero raider; Lorena refuses to go to the cow camp and waits for Jake.
  1. Lorena asks Gus to take her to California; he says he's bound for Ogallala and Clara, offers a train ticket from Denver, and leaves; she stays angry.

  - *The pool* | morning | sunny, pretty | Lorena: Lorena puts her head under the cool pool water and opens her eyes, then feels silly and just washes her hair.
  - *Under the tree by the pool* | morning | very still | Augustus, Lorena: Gus strokes her cheek and holds her hand while the horses swish their tails.
  - *Pool and a big rock* | morning | still, sunny | Augustus, Lorena: Gus splashes across the cold pool in faded pink underwear, then dries on a rock and squints west at a distant glint of silver.
  - *Camp and pool* | morning | sunny | Blue Duck, Augustus, Lorena, bay stallion: Blue Duck squats to drink from a cupped hand while his bay stallion wades in; Gus sits in his underwear with hat on and gun belt near.
  - *Camp* | morning | unspecified | Lorena, Augustus: Lorena weeps as Gus wipes her tears, then sits silent and angry on the rock as he rides away.
- **Visual details:** FIRST APPEARANCE: Blue Duck: big man, very large squarish heavy head, big hands, long tangled black hair, no hat but a bandana around his head, greasy leather leggings, old boots, silver spurs with big rowels, big knife strapped to one leg, rifle across the pommel, heavy voice · FIRST APPEARANCE: Blue Duck's horse: a bay stallion, a pacer, with a silver-decorated Mexican saddle (Gus guesses stolen from a dead Mexican) · Gus's flannel underwear once pink, faded nearly white and full of holes, white chest hair poking through · Gus's heavy old pistol, needs two hands for Lorena to lift the belt · Lorena's wet hair; rolling empty plain to the west
- **Notes:** FIRST APPEARANCE: Blue Duck and his bay stallion. Gus introduces Lorena as 'Miss Wood'. Places named: north of the Canadian (Blue Duck's threat), the Red River country from New Mexico to Arkansas, Galveston, Ogallala, Nebraska, Denver, California, San Francisco, Fort Worth ('Texas don't last much north of' it), Lonesome Dove, Alabama. Blue Duck says he heard of McCrae; Gus guesses Charles Goodnight ran him off; Mackenzie failed to catch him. Lorena asks for 'the black man' (Deets) as guard, not Dish. TheBestNotes says Gus hugs her and 'bathes in the creek' (text: pool) and adds foreshadowing not in this chapter.

  - *Maggie's room over the saloon, Lonesome Dove* | night | unspecified | Call, Maggie: (memory) At the top of the back stairs a small, anxious woman fiddling with her unruly hair asks Call to say her name.
  - *Top of a low limestone bluff* | night | clear, breeze, big moon | Call, Hell Bitch: Call cleans his rifle on his bedroll at the bluff edge, the campfire wick far below, then sleeps with the mare's rope tied round his waist.

  - *open ground between the live oaks and the tie-up boulder* | night | unspecified | Newt: Woken by drumming hooves, Newt runs as fifty or sixty stampeding cattle pour past him toward the bluffs, then finds his horse gone from the boulder and weeps.

Mouse was not found by Newt; Pea says the horse was found mixed in with the cattle, so Mouse is recovered off-page. GradeSaver says Pea Eye 'rides up with Mouse' - the text does not show that; Pea only reports the horse turned up in the herd, and Newt rides in behind Pea.   - *cow camp by the wagon* | morning | unspecified | Po Campo, Newt, Augustus, Jasper, whole crew, the blue pigs: Po Campo lays two branding irons across firewood as a grill and scrambles sixty-odd plover eggs in a huge skillet while the blue pigs eat the shells.
  - *cow camp around the fire* | night | unspecified (mosquitoes about) | Po Campo, Deets, Newt, Call, Pea Eye, Dish, Jasper, Lippy on the wagon seat, others: Po Campo flicks crisp fried grasshoppers onto cheesecloth; Deets dips one in molasses, crunches it and grins, and the crew clears the lot.
