Gus mentions having left his biscuit ovens behind and 'lost the porch'. Blue Duck's earlier threat referenced: he would cut their balls off if they came north of the Canadian. Call suspects Blue Duck started the stampede deliberately. Jake says Blue Duck's gang was talked of in Fort Worth. Jake was gambling in Austin, which puts the camp within a ride of Austin. GradeSaver says Gus 'goes off to look for Lorena' and returns - actually he just goes to the remuda to catch his horse and comes back to saddle it. GradeSaver says Call 'kicks Jake out'; in the text Call tells him to sit down or get out and Jake chooses to leave. Shmoop says Jake 'heads back to town'; text only says he rode out mumbling, Call guessing he'll sleep it off. The day after, Gus and Jake are both still absent.

- **Who (on page):** Lorena Wood, Blue Duck, Ermoke, three of Ermoke's Kiowa men (one with a bugle and a jerky laugh), Lorena's mare, the pack-horse, Blue Duck's big sorrel

  1. Her mare and then the pack-horse give out; she rides double behind him and then bareback on a stolen horse, noticing his necklace of human finger bones.
  1. After crossing a broad red-sand river with care to avoid bogging, they meet Ermoke and three Kiowas carrying a scalp lance.
  1. At dusk Blue Duck lets the four Indians rape Lorena, then kicks Ermoke off her when he refuses to stop.

  - *a five-foot-wide creek below a ridge* | dawn | faint light on the ridge, dark in the creek bed | Lorena, Blue Duck: Horses water for five minutes; Lorena, numb and shaking, is too frightened to drink.
  - *a sizable stream beside a small tree* | afternoon | hot, cloudless, blazing sun | Lorena, Blue Duck: Lorena crawls to the water and gulps; Blue Duck hauls her up by the hair and shoves her head under, then naps under a tree on his saddlebags.
  - *tall-grass country and a wide riverbed of red sand* | late afternoon | hot, heat waves shimmering | Lorena, Blue Duck, Ermoke, three Kiowas: Blue Duck picks a careful path across the boggy red sand, spurring his sorrel free twice, while four mounted Indians wait on the far bank.
  - *open prairie beyond the river* | dusk | red afterglow in the western sky; moon late | Lorena, Ermoke, three Kiowas, Blue Duck: Dragged from her horse, Lorena sees the darkening sky between horses' legs while one man plays bugle calls in the grass.
- **Visual details:** Blue Duck: a big man with a very low, deadly voice, carries a rifle; wears a necklace of human finger bones. · Blue Duck's horse: a big sorrel strong enough to carry two riders. · Lorena's mare stumbles and plays out; the pack-horse lasts a day; the replacement horse is narrow-backed, rough-gaited, ridden bareback with a cord under its belly tying her ankles. · Lorena has yellow hair (Blue Duck says Monkey John will like it); she leaves behind her little box with her comb. · Landscape progression: many creeks, rocky hills and ridges, fewer trees, then flat country with grass higher than she had ever seen and a mirage of a giant tree. · The river: broad bed of reddish sand, only a thin ribbon of brown water, bog holes. · Ermoke: hard face, a thin wisp of mustache hair curling out from each corner of his mouth; carries a lance hung with scalps, one fresh with glossy black hair and dried blood. · The four riders are all Indians, heavily armed, smelling rank; one has a bugle and a jerky laugh.
- **Notes:** The red-sand river is not named here; ch56 states Blue Duck actually crossed the Red and headed due north, so it is the Red River (Texas/Indian Territory border). The creeks and hills are unnamed; direction northwest from the herd is taken from Gus's tracking in ch54. FIRST APPEARANCE: Ermoke and his Kiowa band; Blue Duck's finger-bone necklace; Blue Duck's big sorrel is described as such for the first time here. Monkey John is first named (off-page). Blue Duck predicts 'the old Ranger' (Gus) will follow. Shmoop says Blue Duck withheld water and nearly drowned her; the text has Lorena herself fail to drink at the first stop, and the dunking is framed as him giving her a bath. GradeSaver says Roscoe 'calls Janey to join them' successfully and that July 'arms her' - she ignored the call and only appeared later; July handed her the shotgun just to hold. GradeSaver's 'family of 16' fits (14 children plus parents).

  - *the Canadian valley and the road of bones* | dawn | gray sky, black plains, then sunlight on grass | Augustus, Aus Frank: Woken by bones clattering down the pile, Gus rides east along a line of bones stretching across the plain while Aus trundles his barrow toward the river.

- **When:** Spring - morning described as springlike; weeks into the drive.

- **Key events:**

  1. Dish confides in Newt that Jake is to blame for losing both Gus and Lorena and vows he will still marry her; Newt reluctantly revises his boyhood hero-worship of Jake.

  - *at the point of the herd* | day | iron-grey skies, rain pouring off hat brims | Dish Boggett, Newt Dobbs: Dish, water streaming off his hat, tells Newt he still means to marry Lorena.
  - *low banks of the Red River, wide stretch of wet rusty sand* | day | still raining, then rain slackens and sun breaks through | Woodrow Call, Deets, Dish Boggett, Soupy Jones, Bert Borum, Old Dog: Deets vanishes downriver into the rain and returns with news of a gravel bar; Old Dog leads the herd across in sunshine while Dish and Soupy strip to wade in the mud and rope out bogged cows.
  - *camp on the long wet grass north of the Red* | night | clear | Allen O'Brien, Po Campo, Pea Eye Parker, Soupy Jones, Needle Nelson, Bert Borum: The Irishman sings for hours and Po Campo sings sad Spanish songs, shaking a gourd rattle.

- **Visual details:** Rolling plain bare of trees; nothing to shelter under. · Red River: not wide or deep, but approached over more than a hundred yards of wet, rusty-coloured sand; the water is brownish; the Red is known for quicksand. · Deets waves the others back with his hat before loping downstream. · A gravel bar makes the river entrance almost like a road. · Five or six weak cows bog coming out; Dish and Soupy strip and wade in, Bert Borum hauls them out with ropes. · North bank: long wet grass of the Oklahoma Territory. · Po Campo has a gourd rattle, a low throaty voice, deep-set eyes, and a big-brimmed hat he rarely removes. · Deets keeps a whittled wooden woman from Po Campo in the pocket of his old chaps. · Call ties the Hell Bitch's long rope under his belt and around his wrist at night; she wakes him if she lifts her head at a deer or wolf.
- **Notes:** Exact crossing point on the Red is not named (the text does not mention Doan's store). Dish recalls bogging sixty of Mr. Pierce's cattle in the Red further east toward Arkansas. The Hell Bitch is named in this chapter. Old Dog leads the crossing. Deets and Po Campo are the crew's weather experts.

- **Storyline:** Gus's pursuit
- **Where:** Adobe Walls - deserted, mostly ruined trading post/fort on the Texas Panhandle plains (site of the buffalo hunters' battle about two years earlier); open prairie along the Canadian River, Texas Panhandle; ridge above the Canadian where four trail herds wait for the river to fall; Gus and Lorena's tent camp on a long ridge about two miles north of the Canadian River; San Francisco (Lorena's remembered goal); Fort Worth, Denver, the Purgatory (Purgatoire) River, Ogallala (mentioned)
- **Who (on page):** Augustus McCrae, Lorena Wood, Wilbarger, Wilbarger's old cook, Wilbarger's pack mule

- **When:** Spring rains; Gus notes the Adobe Walls fight was not two years earlier (the 1874 battle, so roughly spring 1876 by that reckoning, though the novel's dating is loose).
- **Herd:** Hat Creek herd is behind, south of the Canadian (Wilbarger thought them three weeks behind him); Gus waits for it north of the Canadian.
- **Key events:**
  1. Caught by chill rains two days east of the buffalo hunters' camp, Gus shelters with Lorena for two days in a roofed room at deserted Adobe Walls.
  1. Gus finds a woman's box of buttons, deals cards for button stakes, and Lorena speaks for the first time since her rescue.
  1. Following the high Canadian, they find four herds waiting for the river to drop; Wilbarger rides out, feeds them, lends a tent and shares a bottle with Gus, and learns July Johnson's companions were killed by Blue Duck.
  1. After the herds cross, Gus and Lorena cross the lowered Canadian and camp two miles north for about a week, playing cards as rain returns.
  1. On an evening at the river Lorena bathes, is shocked at how pale her body is, and breaks down sobbing; Gus comforts her.
- **Scenes (visual beats):**
  - *ruined Adobe Walls, a room with an intact roof* | evening/night over two days | chill steady rain; leak drips down one wall | Augustus McCrae, Lorena Wood: Gus pokes an owl's nest from the fireplace, burns an old wagon, and they play cards on a blanket for buttons sorted by size.
  - *outside the wagon yard at Adobe Walls* | evening, then pre-dawn | rain stops an hour or two before dawn; bright sun on wet prairie | Augustus McCrae, Lorena Wood: Gus shoots a grazing deer; that night Lorena wakes crying and he holds her, then at sunrise she weeps as they mount to leave.
  - *ridge overlooking the Canadian River plain* | toward evening | clear after rain; river high | Augustus McCrae, Lorena Wood, Wilbarger: Four great herds are spread across the plain; Wilbarger gallops out on the bay stolen back from Pedro Flores.
  - *Gus's camp on the treeless plain near Wilbarger's herd* | night, then next morning | fine sunny morning, plains dried out | Augustus McCrae, Wilbarger, Lorena Wood, Wilbarger's old cook, the pack mule: Wilbarger brings beefsteak, beans, a tent and whiskey; Lorena watches through the tent flap; next morning the cook's mule nearly kicks him.
  - *Canadian River bank near the ridge camp two miles north* | pretty evening | fair; rapid water | Augustus McCrae, Lorena Wood: Gus suns himself naked on a rock while Lorena, thigh-deep in the fast river, sees her white belly and brown legs and sobs uncontrollably.
- **Visual details:** Adobe Walls: deserted, buildings mostly in ruins, rattlesnakes, mice and owls; an owl's nest in the fireplace; a wagon yard. · Gus cuts Lorena a crude poncho from a tarp taken at the buffalo hunters' camp. · Box of assorted buttons; large horn coat buttons count as fifty-dollar gold pieces. · Lorena is thinner, cheeks hollowed, bruises healing; she wears a ragged dress. · Wilbarger rides a nice bay horse (formerly stolen from Pedro Flores). · Wilbarger's small pack mule, bad-tempered, carries a pot of beefsteak and beans and a small tent. · The plain around the Canadian is treeless. · Gus's improvised fishing line: coarse thread from Adobe Walls, a bent needle hook, tadpole bait; he catches nothing. · Gus's white hair; Gus strips to bathe whenever at the river. · Wilbarger also gives coffee and a side of bacon.
- **Notes:** FIRST APPEARANCE: the button box (Gus and Lorena's card stakes) and Wilbarger's borrowed tent. Adobe Walls is named explicitly. Gus says July was left four days earlier, headed north. Wilbarger's foreman died south of Fort Worth when a bronc fell backwards on him; Wilbarger asks for the tent to be returned in Denver. Gus's 'unfinished business in Ogallala' is Clara (inferred). Gus believes Blue Duck is heading for the Purgatory River. Study-guide check: GradeSaver compresses the Canadian episode as if Gus and Lorena leave the morning after meeting Wilbarger; the text has them cross the river after the herds and camp about a week on a ridge two miles north before the bath scene. TheBestNotes says Gus had once seen soldiers at Adobe Walls; the text only has him recall the fight and a woman who fled.

- **Storyline:** Hat Creek outfit
- **Where:** empty grass plains of the Indian (Oklahoma) Territory between the Red and the Canadian; open plain half a day south of the Canadian River where lightning strikes the herd; the stampede ground, six or seven miles west of the main herd by dawn; Canadian River - a heavily used herd crossing about four miles from where the herd regrouped, twenty yards of swimming water, six-foot banks; north bank of the Canadian; Dodge City and Ogallala (mentioned in talk)
- **Who (on page):** Newt Dobbs, Woodrow Call, Dish Boggett, Deets, Pea Eye Parker, Allen O'Brien, Bill Spettle, Pete Spettle, Needle Nelson, Jasper Fant, Bert Borum, Po Campo, Mouse, Old Dog, the pigs, Augustus McCrae (seen at a distance)
- **Mentioned:** Lorena Wood, Jake Spoon
- **When:** Spring rains (not otherwise dated).
- **Herd:** Crosses the Canadian River in the Indian Territory (Oklahoma); Red River is behind; Gus and Lorena are camped just north.
- **Key events:**
  1. In the Territory the men fear Indians but see only empty grass and sky; Po Campo gives each cowboy a whittled woman.
  1. Half a day from the Canadian a lightning storm strikes: a bolt kills thirteen cattle, the herd stampedes west, and the crew rides blind through a rainy night.
  1. At dawn Dish brings word that lightning also killed Bill Spettle; he is buried hastily and the herd moves on without Newt seeing the grave.
  1. Call pushes on to the Canadian; the men strip naked to swim the herd across, and a violent hailstorm with egg-sized stones pelts them mid-crossing.
  1. After the hail, the men skip hailstones on the river, Po Campo gathers hail in a bucket, and Pea Eye spots Gus riding toward them.
- **Scenes (visual beats):**
  - *open plain in the Territory* | sunset | lightning flickering in the west all afternoon; a bolt runs south to north across the setting sun | Newt Dobbs, Deets, Woodrow Call: A bolt seems to split the red setting sun, then black clouds roll down like a herd and snuff out the afterglow.
  - *edge of the herd on the open plain* | dusk into night | constant thunder, lightning, then a wall of rain | Newt Dobbs, Pete Spettle, Woodrow Call, Dish Boggett, Deets, Mouse: Lightning drops a row of cattle like bricks a hundred feet from Call; the herd bolts west as bluish light rolls along the horn tips.
  - *wet plain with a thousand strayed cattle* | dawn | low, gloomy, heavily overcast | Newt Dobbs, Dish Boggett, Allen O'Brien, Needle Nelson, Deets: Dish returns with Deets and the news that Bill Spettle was struck dead and is being buried.
  - *Canadian River crossing* | day (after a quick feed) | chilly squall, then heavy hail growing to pullet-egg size | Woodrow Call, Dish Boggett, Newt Dobbs, Needle Nelson, Jasper Fant, Pea Eye Parker, Old Dog, Mouse: Naked riders swim the herd over as hail peppers the water; Newt crouches under Mouse, Jasper hides under his slicker and saddle, Pea Eye stands neck-deep in the river with his hat on.
  - *hail-whitened north bank of the Canadian* | day, after the storm | hail over within ten minutes; plains turned white | Allen O'Brien, Po Campo, the pigs, Needle Nelson, Pea Eye Parker, Jasper Fant, Bert Borum, Augustus McCrae: Men skip hailstones across the river, Po Campo fills a bucket with hail trailed by his two pigs, and a lone rider appears who Pea Eye knows is Gus.
- **Visual details:** Endless grass and sky; occasional wolves and coyotes; no trees. · Each cowboy now owns one of Po Campo's carved wooden women. · Thirteen cattle killed by one bolt; bluish St Elmo's-type light on the horn tips; clicking horns as the cattle run. · Bill Spettle was turned black by the lightning (reported); buried stiff, wrapped in a bedroll. · Pete Spettle holds the remuda together all night, looking weary and stunned. · Men ride naked across the Canadian, clothes and boots wrapped in slickers tied high on saddles; bodies fish-belly white except brown hands and faces; Deets is one colour. · Dish holds his saddle strings as his horse swims; mistakes the first hail for bullets. · Mouse jumps a six-foot bank into the river, throwing Newt. · Hailstones cover the muddy banks and plain; cattle and horses crunch through them. · Po Campo's two pigs follow him like dogs.
- **Notes:** Bill Spettle dies here (second boy lost after Sean O'Brien). Newt mistakes hail for his first sight of snow. The crossing point is described only as a heavily used herd crossing four miles from the regroup point; geographically this is in western Indian Territory, but the text does not name any town or landmark. Gus is seen 'across the river' riding toward them - slightly odd given his camp is north of the river; ch63 confirms he came to the north bank. The pigs appear (the blue shoat is specified in ch63).

- **Storyline:** July Johnson
- **Where:** empty high plains north of the Canadian River (Texas Panhandle / western Indian Territory); a spot three days north of the Cimarron River where July's horse goes lame (likely southwestern Kansas / Neutral Strip area); a dry camp on brown grass plains; a grove of low trees around a trickling spring with a ten-foot-wide pool; a running creek reached by following a wagon track; the Johns outfit's trail herd; Dodge City, Kansas; remembered: Fort Smith, the family cabin in Arkansas, the graves on the Canadian

- **Herd:** not stated in chapter
- **Key events:**
  1. Grieving the three dead buried on the Canadian, July rides north for days through empty country, eating mostly jackrabbit.
  1. Three days north of the Cimarron a hidden cactus thorn lames his horse; he eventually shoots it and walks east with his rifle, weeping at the buzzards over it.
  1. Out of water at a dry camp, he spends a moonlit night contemplating suicide with his pistol but decides he must first find Elmira and tell her about Joe.
  1. Crows lead him to a spring in a grove of trees where he drinks, washes, and eats a badger he shoots.

Wilbarger is wounded (Dan claims three hits) and escapes; his fate is left open here. Frog Lip dies. Chick is identified as a hand seen when the Hat Creek men returned Wilbarger's horses from Mexico (Pedro Flores). Dan says Wilbarger has probably sold his lead herd in Dodge and has other herds bound for Denver. The settlers killed here are not named. Study-guide check: GradeSaver says Dan shoots Frog Lip; in the text Dan refuses and little Eddie shoots him in the head (TheBestNotes' vaguer 'they shoot' is consistent).

- **Storyline:** Hat Creek outfit
- **Where:** Hat Creek herd's crossing of the Arkansas River, Kansas, at a shallow, low-banked ford west of Dodge City (Gus doubts any herd has been trailed this far west of Dodge); Open grassland a few miles north of the Arkansas where the herd grazes that afternoon; Wilbarger's dying place: under the bank of the Arkansas about ten miles to the east/southeast of the herd; Wilbarger's grave on the bare Arkansas riverbank, marked with a cow-buffalo skull
- **Who (on page):** Deets, Call, Augustus McCrae, Dish Boggett, Newt, Lorena, Pea Eye, Wilbarger, Po Campo, Lippy, Rainey boys

- **When:** Midsummer - stated directly ('though it was midsummer, the skies clear').
- **Herd:** Crossing the Arkansas River in Kansas, west of Dodge City; by midafternoon a few miles north of the Arkansas on ungrazed buffalo grass. Next major river: the Republican.
- **Key events:**
  1. Deets finds Wilbarger's big bay, blood-crusted, waiting on the north bank as the herd crosses the Arkansas; Dish's horse panics mid-river and Deets saves Dish, and the herd scatters into five or six bunches.
  1. Deets backtracks the bay and reports Wilbarger shot three times by white horse thieves about ten miles off.
  1. Call leaves Dish in charge of the herd and of Lorena, and rides east with Gus, Deets, Pea Eye and Newt.
  1. Wilbarger names Dan Suggs and his brothers as his killers, bequeaths Gus his Milton and Virgil and the tent, gives his horse to Gus and asks his remaining horses be sold for his brother, then dies after dark.
  1. They bury him on the bare bank with a cow-buffalo skull Deets fetches as a marker; Newt is given Wilbarger's rifle and Gus talks to him about the bones under the plains.
- **Scenes (visual beats):**
  - *Arkansas River crossing, Kansas* | morning | clear (midsummer, skies clear) | Deets, Dish Boggett, Newt, Call, Augustus McCrae, Old Dog: Deets swims over ahead of Old Dog to catch a riderless bloody bay while Dish's horse thrashes in midstream and hundreds of cattle drift away downstream.
  - *grassland a few miles north of the Arkansas* | afternoon | clear, hot | Call, Augustus McCrae, Lorena, Deets: Deets comes loping along the river from the southeast on a sweat-streaked horse with news that Wilbarger is shot and dying.
  - *Lorena's tent by the wagon* | late afternoon | hot | Augustus McCrae, Lorena, Dish Boggett, Lippy, Po Campo, Call, Newt: Gus tells a shaking Lorena he must go and puts a dazzled Dish in charge of her, while Po Campo sings over supper and Newt swells with pride at being chosen.
  - *under the bank of the Arkansas, about ten miles east* | dusk into night | long plains twilight, then full dark | Wilbarger, Call, Augustus McCrae, Deets, Pea Eye, Newt: Wilbarger lies on a blanket bleeding and joking with Gus while Newt stands weeping by the horses and Deets keeps watch on the riverbank.
  - *Wilbarger's grave on the bare riverbank* | night | unspecified | Augustus McCrae, Deets, Pea Eye, Call, Newt: Deets sets a bleached cow-buffalo skull on the fresh grave and Gus rides beside Newt through the night talking of bones in the earth.
- **Visual details:** Wilbarger's horse: the big bay he rode into Lonesome Dove, dried blood on the saddle and crusted in its mane · Call rides the Hell Bitch (the mare); Wilbarger admires her from his deathbed · Wilbarger lies on a blanket Deets left him in the shade of the riverbank; shot in the lung and hip plus a flesh wound · The Arkansas at this ford is fairly shallow with low banks; the plains and banks are bare, with no bushes or anything to mark a grave · Grave marker: the skull of a cow buffalo from bones Deets had seen · Wilbarger's saddlebags hold a Milton and a Virgil · Call packs two boxes of rifle shells; Newt keeps touching his pistol · Lorena's small tent (lent by Wilbarger); she closes its flaps despite the heat · Lippy walks a mile out on the treeless plain for privacy · Wilbarger's rifle passed to Newt
- **Notes:** Wilbarger's location: Deets returns 'along the river from the southeast' and the party rides east to reach him ~10 miles away, so he collapsed on the Arkansas downstream (east) of the crossing - between the herd and Dodge. Wilbarger was born on the Hudson, went to Yale, brother John Wilbarger at Fifty Broadway, New York City; he fought for the Union. FIRST APPEARANCE in this range of the buffalo-skull grave marker; Wilbarger's rifle becomes Newt's. The Hat Creek sign is recalled by Wilbarger (he praises Gus's sign). Study-guide discrepancy: GradeSaver says Dish's horse panicked because it smelled blood on Wilbarger's saddle - the text gives no reason, only that it took fright. Wilbarger's black companion who was shot is not named in this chapter (GradeSaver/Shmoop name him Frog Lip from ch71). The horse 'mean plug' Wilbarger gives Gus is presumably the bay.

### Chapter 74

*Opening cue:* “When They Found Wilbarger’s man…” · *Words:* 5,829 · *Est. start (Horsley):* 24:25 · *Confidence:* medium

- **Storyline:** mixed (Hat Creek outfit; Jake & the Suggs)
- **Where:** A small knoll on the Kansas prairie, north/east of the Arkansas, where buzzards cover the bodies of Wilbarger's man Chick and the boy (and the Suggs's dead black companion); Site of the burned settlers, still hanging, a few hours further along the thieves' trail; A steep-banked creek with trees on the rolling Kansas prairie, where the Suggs gang camped by a small patent-medicine wagon; Wilbarger's horses graze three or four miles beyond; The hanging tree beside that creek, and Jake Spoon's grave on the slope above the creek; Dark prairie to the west, back toward the herd
- **Who (on page):** Call, Augustus McCrae, Deets, Pea Eye, Newt, Jake Spoon, Dan Suggs, Roy Suggs, Eddie Suggs (little Eddie)
- **Mentioned:** Wilbarger, Chick, Lorena, Maggie (Newt's mother), Collins (the murdered medicine-show drummer), Wanz, the settler whose wife Jake spoke to
- **When:** Midsummer (day after ch72; same day as ch73's second day). Hangings at dusk, burial by moonlight.
- **Herd:** Not with the herd; the herd is somewhere west, north of the Arkansas in Kansas, easing north under Dish.
- **Key events:**
  1. They bury the buzzard-torn remains of Wilbarger's men and the Suggs's black rider; Deets reads Jake Spoon's sideways-riding track among the four thieves.
  1. They cut down and bury two farmers who were shot, hanged and burned, then Deets locates the gang camped drunk at a creek beside a murdered drummer's wagon.
  1. Late in the afternoon Call, Gus and Deets creep up the creek bed with the sun behind them; Deets wounds little Eddie and the rest are taken without a fight, Dan's boot derringer removed.
  1. At a tree by the creek Deets fixes four nooses; Gus whips Dan's horse away and Eddie's bolts too, Roy follows, and Jake - after bidding his old partners farewell and giving Newt his pacing horse - spurs his own horse out from under himself.
  1. They bury Jake by moonlight under a tailgate marker, leave Dan hanging with a written placard, take the rabbits, medicines, mules and Wilbarger's horses, and drive west with Newt asleep on Jake's pacer.
- **Scenes (visual beats):**
  - *buzzard-covered knoll on the prairie* | morning | fine, bright day | Call, Augustus McCrae, Pea Eye, Newt, Deets: A fat badger trots off with a black human hand, a coyote bursts from hundreds of buzzards, and Gus shoots two birds before they bury the remains.
  - *the hanging settlers, further along the trail* | afternoon | unspecified | Call, Augustus McCrae, Pea Eye, Newt, Deets: A coyote tugs at a charred body swinging from a rope until the riders drive it off and cut the farmers down.
  - *steep-banked creek with the Suggs camp and the medicine wagon* | late afternoon | sun low and blinding | Call, Augustus McCrae, Deets, Jake Spoon, Dan Suggs, Roy Suggs, Eddie Suggs: Three rifles rise over the creek bank with the sun behind them, Deets drops little Eddie, and Gus jams his dragoon Colt into Dan's belly.
  - *tree with four nooses by the creek* | dusk | twilight deepening | Call, Augustus McCrae, Deets, Pea Eye, Newt, Jake Spoon, Dan Suggs, Roy Suggs, Eddie Suggs: Four bound men sit horses under a limb; Dan and Eddie swing first, then Jake says goodbye and spurs his pacer so the rope creaks on the bark.
  - *slope above the creek, then the dark prairie heading west* | night (moonlight) | moonlit | Call, Augustus McCrae, Deets, Pea Eye, Newt: Call scratches Jake's name on a tailgate board by lantern light, then the party drives Wilbarger's horses west while Pea Eye rides close beside the sleeping Newt.
- **Visual details:** Hundreds of buzzards standing on the prairie like a black army while others circle · Pea Eye's little shovel · Burned settlers: shreds of charred clothes clinging to the hanging bodies · The creek has high, steep banks that hide the approach; three horses water in it · Small wagon on the near bank; driver's corpse about fifty yards away; the gang shoots at buzzards approaching it · Wagon contents: patent medicines, cheaply printed show circulars, a good lantern, four white rabbits in a cage, a hatchet; pulled by two mules; in poor repair · Augustus's big dragoon Colt; Dan's derringer in his right boot · Jake: several days' stubble, dirty, dull-eyed, drunk, weaving; he rides slightly sideways in the saddle · Jake's horse: a pacing gelding with a smooth gait, plus his fine saddle - both go to Newt; stirrups too long until Deets shortens them · Dan Suggs: snarling, bared teeth, quivering with rage · Placard on Dan's body written on the back of a circular: 'Man Burner and Horse Thief' · Jake's grave marker made from the broken wagon tailgate, name scratched with a pocketknife · Deets and Gus each carry two white rabbits in their saddlebags; Gus takes bottles of patent medicine · Call on the Hell Bitch holds a slow lope all day
- **Notes:** WHERE JAKE AND THE SUGGSES ARE HANGED: Kansas (Gus jokes they must be stopped 'before they wipe out Kansas'; Eddie says Dan claimed no man in Kansas could take him; Jake says he was only trying to get to Kansas). The creek is unnamed; it lies north of the Arkansas and roughly east of the herd (the party rides east in ch72 and afterwards drives the horses west to rejoin). No river is named for the hanging site - do not map it to the Arkansas itself. Collins's wagon was 'headed for Denver, I guess' per Call. Jake had about $800 won in Fort Worth poker. FIRST APPEARANCE: Jake's pacing horse as Newt's mount (Newt's other horse is turned into the herd, still saddled). Dan Suggs left hanging. Study-guide discrepancies: Shmoop says Eddie was shot reaching for his rifle (text: his pistol) and implies Newt's horse had just died (Mouse died earlier; here Newt simply switches horses); GradeSaver names the black rider Frog Lip - the text here calls him only the bad black man. Burials: Jake, Roy, Eddie and Collins; Dan left up.

### Chapter 75

*Opening cue:* “Clara Was Milking A Mare…” · *Words:* 9,695 · *Est. start (Horsley):* 25:01 · *Confidence:* high

- **Storyline:** mixed (Clara; Elmira)
- **Where:** Clara and Bob Allen's horse ranch on the Platte River, Nebraska, about twenty miles from Ogallala (on the south side, per ch77): a two-story frame house with an upper porch, corrals/lots, saddle shed, kindling shed, cistern; The knoll east of the house where Clara's three boys are buried (and a grave dug for Bob); The old sod house Bob dug on a slope above the Platte (memory); The Platte River (Clara washes her hair in it; mists float over it); Ogallala, Nebraska ('a bad town'), twenty miles off; Mentioned: the Dismal River (Cholo caught in an April blizzard), St. Louis (piano), Red River of the North, the Dakotas, Montreal (Jules), Cincinnati (Clara's aunt), Missouri, Wyoming (the light spreading toward it), Texas, Arkansas
- **Who (on page):** Clara Allen, Sally, Betsey, Bob Allen, Cholo, Elmira, Big Zwey, Luke, Elmira's newborn son
- **Mentioned:** Jim, Jeff and Johnny (Clara's dead sons), Jules (French-Canadian piano teacher), Maude Jones and Len Jones, Augustus McCrae, Dee Boot, July (unnamed; 'a sheriff'), Clara's aunt in Cincinnati
- **When:** Summer (summer breezes through Bob's window; Bob injured three months earlier). Elmira's arrival predates July's by three weeks (ch77).
- **Herd:** Not stated in chapter (herd is still in Kansas at this time, per surrounding chapters).
- **Key events:**
  1. Clara, milking a mare to save a weak foal, is told by her daughters that a wagon is coming along the Platte; she sends them to bathe their father, who lies paralysed and mute three months after a mustang mare kicked him behind the ear.
  1. Through Clara's memories: the sod house and its dirt, the deaths of her three boys, the piano from St. Louis and Jules the teacher, Bob's quitting whiskey, Cholo's skill and gentleness, and her longing to write.
  1. Elmira, Luke and Big Zwey arrive; Elmira asks for Dee Boot (Cholo calls him a pistolero), says they came from Arkansas, and collapses.
  1. Through the night and morning Elmira labours; Cholo delivers a tiny boy and breathes life into him, but Elmira will not look at the child.
  1. The next night Elmira leaves with the hunters, abandoning the baby; Clara feeds him boiled milk from a rag and holds him beside the unresponsive Bob.
- **Scenes (visual beats):**
  - *horse lots below the house* | afternoon | unspecified (summer) | Clara Allen, Sally, Betsey, Cholo: Clara squirts milk down her own arm as Sally runs up shouting about a wagon creeping along the Platte from the west.
  - *the icy kindling shed (memory)* | unspecified | below-zero winter wind along the Platte | Clara Allen, Bob Allen: (memory) Clara sits by two small bodies wrapped in wagon sheets, tears frozen on her cheeks, waiting for the ground to thaw.
  - *path between house and barn* | late afternoon | unspecified | Clara Allen, Elmira, Big Zwey, Luke, Cholo: Heavily pregnant Elmira climbs down from a buffalo-hide wagon, sits, topples over, and Zwey carries her to the house like a child.
  - *upstairs bedroom and Clara's upper porch* | night into dawn | beautiful morning light spreading toward Wyoming | Clara Allen, Cholo, Elmira, Bob Allen: Elmira screams through the night; at morning Cholo eases out a limp baby boy and blows on him until he cries.
  - *Clara's upper porch and Bob's bedside* | night (moonlight) | moonlit | Clara Allen, Cholo, the baby, Bob Allen: With the wagon gone north of the corrals, Clara dribbles boiled milk into the baby from a rag, then feeds Bob soup with the infant at the foot of his bed.
- **Visual details:** Sally, 10, and Betsey, 7: bright blue eyes like Bob's, brown hair and knobby knees like Clara's · Clara: gray eyes, washes and brushes her hair daily; sleeps on a cot on the upper porch · Bob: big man, over 200 pounds, now wasting, ribs showing, large blue eyes staring, bed moved by the window · Two-story frame house (timber hauled 200 miles), piano from St. Louis, windows the girls watch from · Old sod house: dirt sifting from the roof, muslin and canvas tacked over the bed, centipedes · Cholo: white hair, perhaps 75-80, arrow scars, weaves horsehair lariats from dead horses' manes and tails; owns a buffalo coat Clara bought him · Jules: black coat, waxed mustache · Shotgun kept in the saddle shed for egg-stealing snakes; hens Clara talks to · Arriving party: a big man in a heavy coat on a small tired brown horse, a man with a badly scarred face on the wagon seat, a heavily pregnant woman driving; a few buffalo hides in the wagon · Newborn: red, then pale, under five pounds, wrapped in a flannel robe · Mists over the Platte in the mornings; flowers Clara plants that the north wind strips
- **Notes:** FIRST APPEARANCE on-page: Clara Allen, Bob Allen, Sally, Betsey, Cholo, and the ranch; FIRST APPEARANCE of Elmira's baby (later named Martin). Part III begins here. Clara's ranch is 20 miles from Ogallala on the Platte; the direction from town is not stated here - ch77 puts the house half a mile south of the Platte on the river road, and ch83's 'man who sells horses west of Ogallala' implies it lies west of town. Oddly, the wagon is said to come along the Platte from the west. Text inconsistency: Bob's accident is 'three months' ago in one place and he has lain 'two months' in another. Elmira and the hunters leave the night after the birth (not the evening of arrival, as GradeSaver implies). Luke has a twisted leg from chasing an antelope. Cholo was stolen as a child by Comanches. Bob's main customer is the Army. Clara's reading: Blackwood's, Thackeray, Dickens, George Eliot, Mrs. Gore, Mrs. Gaskell, Charlotte Yonge, Scott's Ivanhoe.

- **Notes:** CAPTAIN WEAVER and DIXON first appear here (Dixon will matter in Ogallala). The text does NOT name the dead - it strongly implies Big Zwey and Elmira (who left Ogallala eastward), but that is an inference; GradeSaver and Shmoop state it as fact. Deets's premonitory mood (dislikes the north, 'thin' light, stares south) begins here. GradeSaver also says Weaver 'reveals that American Indians killed Elmira and Zwey' - text only says a buffalo hunter and a woman.

- **Where:** The Platte River west of Ogallala, trailed for several days until it curves south toward Colorado; Herd turns northwest into Wyoming: sandy, sage and tuft-grass plain with small alkaline streams; a black line of mountains far to the south; The last stream before the dry stretch (camp where Gus bathes); Roughly eighty waterless miles of white, sandy plain to Salt Creek (Wyoming); Salt Creek: not running, water in scattered shallow pools; the herd reaches it upstream of where Call first struck it; Mountains visible to the north, called the Rocky Mountains by Gus; Powder River and Yellowstone named as the route ahead; Remembered/mentioned: the Canadian River and New Mexico (Po Campo's sons' graves), the llano, the Columbia River gorge, the Pecos, San Antonio, Fort Phantom Hill
- **Who (on page):** Woodrow F. Call, Augustus McCrae, Dish Boggett, Newt Dobbs, Po Campo, Lippy Jones, Deets, Pea Eye Parker, Jasper Fant, Needle Nelson, Soupy Jones, Allen O'Brien, Ben Rainey, Jimmy Rainey, the Texas bull, Gus's two pigs, the Hell Bitch
- **Mentioned:** Lorena Wood, Clara Allen, Blue Duck, Po Campo's dead sons, Jim Bridger, Jake Spoon, O'Brien's wife in Ireland, Ben (Call's old mule)
- **When:** High summer (stated): blazing hot days; later dust and heat 'as hot as south Texas'.
- **Herd:** Left the Platte where it curves south toward Colorado; crossed ~80 dry miles of Wyoming; at Salt Creek by chapter's end. Next: the Powder River, then the Yellowstone (about three weeks, per Call).
- **Key events:**

  1. Deets finds no water ahead; Call rides the Hell Bitch about 60 miles to Salt Creek's pools and straight back, reporting water some 80 miles off, and decides to push on.
  1. Driving through the night and day, the herd is hit by a hot sandstorm at a blood-red sunset; cattle scatter until it dies after three hours.
  1. In brutal heat the cattle go thirst-blind and the Irishman grows delirious; the sleepless Call dozes in the saddle and wakes to find Deets leading his mare.
  1. The herd smells water and trots into Salt Creek, losing only six head; mountains appear to the north and Call says three weeks up the Powder to the Yellowstone.

  - *last stream before the dry stretch* | afternoon | hot | Augustus McCrae, Dish Boggett, Soupy Jones, Deets, Pea Eye Parker: Gus sits naked in the shallow stream splashing his long white hair while cowboys holding the herd stare; Deets trims Pea Eye's horse's hoof.

The water they reach is Salt Creek, not the Powder: BestNotes wrongly says they reach and rest by the Powder; GradeSaver and Shmoop say 'Salt River' but the text says Salt Creek. Call's plan: strike Salt Creek to the west and follow it to the Powder. The mountains to the north are named only as 'the Rocky Mountains' by Gus (in reality likely the Bighorns, named in ch.91). The 'black line of mountains' to the south is unnamed. Losses: six head. The gray wolf reappears; Call thinks it may be the same one seen after the picnic.

  - *living room after the funeral* | night | unspecified | Reverend Spinnow, Clara Allen, July Johnson, Martin, Lorena Wood, Sally Allen: Clara rocks the colicky baby as the drunk minister rolls off the sofa and snores on the floor; later she holds sobbing Sally upstairs.

- **Storyline:** Hat Creek outfit
- **Where:** Entering Montana from the barren Wyoming plain: long rolling tall-grass plains with yellow flowers; Riding for days alongside the Bighorn Mountains, peaks sometimes in cloud; Open plains stretching north toward the Yellowstone (not yet crossed); Mentioned: Miles City (somewhere ahead, for whiskey); Tennessee (Gus's boyhood); Kansas (where Newt lost his jacket); Texas

- **Mentioned:** Deets, Jake Spoon, Custer, Gus's father and sisters in Tennessee, Newt's mother
- **When:** Late summer / early autumn (inferred): cool crisp mornings, cold nights, an early snow off the Bighorns, followed by a hot week.
- **Herd:** Just entered Montana from Wyoming, travelling north along the Bighorn Mountains; the Yellowstone not yet reached or crossed; Miles City somewhere ahead.
- **Key events:**
  1. The herd moves into Montana; cool air, deep blue skies, elk, deer, antelope and buffalo lift Call's spirits and he concedes Jake was right about Montana, though he wants the first ranch north of the Yellowstone.
  1. An early storm off the Bighorns drops a thin snow overnight; Pete Spettle won't leave his blankets, Newt fights to pull boots over wet socks until Po Campo dusts them with flour.
  1. Gus teases that the pigs are the first to walk from Texas to Montana.
  1. Scouting fifteen miles ahead with Newt, Gus sees a grizzly far upwind and then tells Newt plainly that Call is his father.
  1. Newt is left confused and let down, feeling the news has spoiled Montana, as they lope back toward the ant-sized herd.
- **Scenes (visual beats):**
  - *rolling Montana grassland beside the Bighorn Mountains* | midday | cool, deep blue sky with white clouds to the north | Woodrow F. Call, Augustus McCrae, the herd: The herd crosses tall grass and yellow flowers as Call and Gus argue about stopping and about the Yellowstone.
  - *night camp on the plains* | dawn | thin snow after an icy night storm, then bright sun | Pete Spettle, Newt Dobbs, Pea Eye Parker, Rainey boys, Po Campo: Cowboys crowd a fire on snowy ground; Pea Eye eats snow, the Raineys pelt each other with snowballs, and Po Campo flours Newt's boots.
  - *trail behind the wagon* | unspecified | hot again | Po Campo, Gus's two pigs: Po Campo walks behind the wagon followed by the pigs tunnelling through the tall grass like moles.
  - *open plain fifteen miles ahead of the herd* | morning | cloudless | Augustus McCrae, Newt Dobbs, a grizzly (distant): Gus, rifle across his saddle and long white hair to his shoulders, tells Newt that Call is his pa while the herd shows as tiny dots behind them.
- **Visual details:** Tall grass sprinkled with yellow flowers; very long views · Bighorn peaks sometimes hidden in cloud · Herd of elk; small groups of buffalo; deer and antelope · Thin snow blanket that melts under bright sun; men wrap in blankets on night herd · Newt has no jacket (lost in Kansas); tight boots, wet socks · Gus carries his big rifle propped across his saddle; white hair nearly shoulder length · Cattle look like black dots / ants from miles away
- **Notes:** The exact point of entering Montana is not named; it is described as leaving the Wyoming plain. FIRST explicit statement to Newt that Call is his father (hinted in ch.90). GradeSaver says Augustus 'does not enjoy being in Montana and wishes to return to Texas'; the text has him complain of missing Texas and whiskey but then tell Newt he wouldn't have missed it and calls it rare country - a partial misreading. Gus left Tennessee at thirteen.

### Chapter 94

*Opening cue:* “The Men Began To Talk…” · *Words:* 11,651 · *Est. start (Horsley):* 32:23 · *Confidence:* high

- **Storyline:** Hat Creek outfit
- **Where:** Hat Creek camp on the plains south of the Yellowstone River, Montana Territory (a day and a half's ride south of the river); Yellowstone River crossing where buffalo were watering (swift, cold, not deep); Open rolling prairie north of the Yellowstone, three or more days' ride north, short of the Milk River; Unnamed creek with trees and brush, later a steep ten-foot cutbank upstream where Gus and Pea dig a cave (identified in ch96 by Call as the Musselshell River); Open plains between the creek and the Yellowstone that Pea Eye crosses on foot; Herd camp just north of the Yellowstone (crossed the day before Pea is found)
- **Who (on page):** Augustus McCrae, Pea Eye Parker, Captain Woodrow Call, Jasper Fant, Dish Boggett, Lippy Jones, Po Campo, Newt Dobbs, Bert Borum, Needle Nelson, Soupy Jones, unnamed Indian war party (about twenty), Indian boy of about seventeen killed by Gus
- **Mentioned:** Deets (dead, buried on the Powder; Pea believes his ghost guides him), Jake Spoon (described the Milk and Marais), Barlow (old acquaintance hooked by a buffalo), Widow Cole, Kit Carson, Custer, the Irishman (Allen O'Brien, whose singing Pea listens for), the old blind Comanche who taught Gus a war cry
- **When:** Late summer turning to autumn: the men fear winter, a cloud bank brings talk of snow, and Pea is caught in a wet snow squall one night followed by a blazing hot day two days later. No month named.
- **Herd:** At the start the herd is still south of the Yellowstone (Gus reckons over a hundred miles from the fight site and not yet at the river). By the end the herd crossed the Yellowstone the day before Pea is found, and Call orders it moved on north. Miles City is the nearest town.
- **Key events:**
  1. The crew frets about the Yellowstone and about wintering so far north; Call sends Gus, with Pea Eye, to scout for ranch land beyond the river.
  1. Gus and Pea cross the Yellowstone, Gus chases buffalo for sport, and three days farther north Gus rides over a rise into a war party of about twenty Indians and takes two arrows in his left leg.
  1. Holed up in a creek bed, they lose both horses to a knife-wielding Indian, Gus kills several attackers, and they dig a cave under a steep bank where Gus forces the deep arrow through his leg and faints.
  1. A cloudburst floods the creek; with Gus feverish, he sends Pea to swim downstream at night and fetch Call, and Pea loses his clothes, rifle and food in the current.
  1. Pea walks naked and barefoot south for about three days, feeling Deets beside him, until Dish spots him north of the herd; Call puts Dish in charge and rides north alone to find Gus.
- **Scenes (visual beats):**
  - *Hat Creek camp on the plain* | morning | breezy, dark cloud bank in the northwest, talk of snow | Augustus McCrae, Pea Eye Parker, Jasper Fant, Call, Dish Boggett: Gus stuffs extra ammunition in his saddlebags and lopes off with Pea while the melancholy crew watches; they pass Po Campo hunting wild onions a mile out.
  - *Yellowstone River and the prairie beyond* | unspecified | clear blue sky after the clouds blew away | Augustus McCrae, Pea Eye Parker: Fifty or sixty buffalo scatter from the river; Gus drinks cold water from his hands mid-crossing, then races alongside the running herd just for the joy of it.
  - *Grassy rise and open prairie north of the Yellowstone* | morning | crisp early, then sunny and warm | Augustus McCrae, Pea Eye Parker, Indian war party: Gus comes racing back down a slope with twenty-odd mounted Indians firing guns and arrows behind him; the pair gallop several miles to a brushy creek.
  - *Creek bend with a sheer ten-foot bank; shallow cave dug beneath it* | dusk into night, then a stormy dawn | still at dusk; later thunder, lightning and a long hard cold rain that floods the creek | Augustus McCrae, Pea Eye Parker, Indians: Arrows rain over the bank, Pea cuts the arrowhead free as Gus faints, war cries echo and Gus answers with a Comanche yell; the brown flood rises to within yards of the cave.
  - *Open plains south toward the Yellowstone; the herd's camp* | night swim; days of walking; found two hours after sunup | cloudy dark night, a wet snow squall, then a blazing hot day | Pea Eye Parker, Dish Boggett, Call, Po Campo, crew: Naked, sunburned and with swollen bleeding feet, Pea sits in the grass talking to the unseen Deets until Dish and Call find him and wrap him in Call's slicker.
- **Visual details:** Jasper has made himself crude floats from empty lard buckets for fear of deep rivers. · Pea sharpens his knife before any trip; he ties his bedroll on and carries a rifle. · Fifty or sixty buffalo at the Yellowstone; smaller bunches of twenty or thirty farther north; the pair eat the liver of a crippled buffalo calf while buzzards and prairie wolves wait. · The prairie north of the river is brown in the distance with grass waving in the breeze. · The Indians' horses are poorer than the Hat Creek horses; attackers use both guns and arrows and shelter behind their horses out of range. · Gus fires his big Henry rifle from under his propped-up saddle and drops five or six Indian horses. · Both scouts' horses are found with throats cut, bright blood on green weeds. · Gus takes an old cap-and-ball pistol and an old Sharps carbine from the dead Indian boy; the carbine stock serves as a shovel. · Gus plasters his wound with a cannonball-sized handful of creek mud. · Pea's shoulder is shot clean through above the shoulder blade; he ends up stark naked, sunburned, feet swollen and cut, blood on his chest. · Call is shoeing the mare (the Hell Bitch), who must be snubbed to allow it; he lends Pea his slicker and Dish buttons it for him. · Po Campo spoon-feeds Pea coffee against the wagon.
- **Notes:** The attackers are never identified in this chapter (Gus jokes they did not introduce themselves); ch95 has Hugh Auld name them Blood Indians. The fight is NOT on the Yellowstone itself but on a creek three-plus days north of it and short of the Milk; in ch96 Call calls it the Musselshell River (real-world the Musselshell lies west of Miles City and flows north to the Missouri, so the novel's geography is loose). GradeSaver says about 40 warriors; text says at least twenty. TheBestNotes calls the attackers Utes, which the text never does. Gus's arrows are in his LEFT leg. Gus's kill count: three from the bank, the Indian boy and a second man in the brush, then one on the far bank in the flood (Hugh later hears seven). Deets is placed as buried on the Powder River. Pea recalls the grizzly that fought the Texas bull. The crew boasts of being the first to bring a Texas herd across the Yellowstone. Gus carries his own big Henry rifle here (not to be confused with Call's Henry, which Call gives Newt in ch100).

### Chapter 95

*Opening cue:* “Augustus Kept His Pistol Cocked…” · *Words:* 2,068 · *Est. start (Horsley):* 33:33 · *Confidence:* high

- **Storyline:** Hat Creek outfit
- **Where:** Cave under the creek bank where Gus was besieged (the Musselshell per Call in ch96), north of the Yellowstone, Montana Territory; Open plain southeast of the creek, on the way to Miles City (about forty miles from where Hugh finds Gus); Slope north of the Yellowstone overlooking Miles City four or five miles to the east; Miles City, Montana Territory: street in front of a saloon
- **Who (on page):** Augustus McCrae, Hugh Auld (Old Hugh), old trapper, Custer, Hugh's spotted horse, men of Miles City outside the saloon
- **Mentioned:** Pea Eye Parker, Captain Call, Clara Allen (seen in delirium), Lorena Wood (in a fever dream of the tent in Kansas), Blood and Blackfoot Indians, General Custer
- **When:** Autumn: Gus notes winter is coming and the Indians need buffalo before it; the dawn is sunny and hot. Month not stated.
- **Herd:** Not stated in chapter; Gus guesses the herd may have moved north faster than he reckoned but expects Call to be days away. Miles City lies on the Yellowstone, southeast of the fight.
- **Key events:**
  1. Feverish and delirious, Gus watches the black water all night for Indians floating down on logs, drifting in thought to Clara.
  1. At sunny dawn, with his leg yellow and black-streaked from blood poisoning, he builds a crutch from the Indian carbine and his cut stirrups and hobbles southeast toward Miles City, finding the Indians gone after buffalo.
  1. He collapses in the night and wakes at noon to find old trapper Hugh Auld squatting over him with a bowie knife, considering taking off the rotten leg.
  1. Hugh names the attackers as Blood Indians, lends Gus his spotted horse Custer, and ties him into the saddle.
  1. Gus reaches Miles City at dark, fires his pistol to summon help, and is thrown into the street by the skittish horse just as he is untied and passes out.
- **Scenes (visual beats):**
  - *Cave under the flooded creek bank* | night | unspecified; creek high and black | Augustus McCrae: Shaking with fever, Gus sits with pistol cocked watching the dark water, seeing Clara's face in delirium.
  - *Receding creek, a game trail up the bank, then open plain* | morning into night | sunny and hot; Gus sweats yet feels chilled | Augustus McCrae: Gus lashes stirrups to the Indian carbine as a crutch and drags himself southeast across the empty plain, crossing fresh tracks of a big buffalo herd, resting standing up.
  - *Open plain where Gus fell* | midday | sun directly overhead | Augustus McCrae, Hugh Auld: (dream of Lorena and a red sun first) Gus wakes to a tiny, bent old man in patched buckskins spitting tobacco and holding a bowie knife, a spotted horse grazing nearby.
  - *Slope north of the Yellowstone, then Miles City street* | sunset to nightfall | unspecified | Augustus McCrae, Miles City men, Custer the horse: Tied to the saddle, Gus fires three shots in front of a saloon; men untie him, the spotted horse bucks him into the street and bolts back out of town.
- **Visual details:** Gus's leg turns yellow with black streaks, and by noon the next day simply black. · Crutch: the Indian boy's carbine with a stirrup lashed over each end, one end padded with saddle leather. · Gus carries one pistol in his belt, one holstered, his rifle, and a pocketful of jerky. · Hugh Auld: very old, scarcely five feet tall, back permanently bent from a deadfall accident, tobacco-stained beard, patched buckskins, bowie knife; says he is probably not yet eighty. · Hugh's horse Custer is spotted, bucks if kicked, bites strangers, and is named for the general Hugh once scouted for. · Hugh ties a rawhide loop around Gus's waist fastened to the cantle. · Gus's delirium: a red world, lying on a swollen red sun sinking into the plain.
- **Notes:** FIRST APPEARANCE: Hugh Auld (Old Hugh) and his spotted horse Custer. Hugh identifies the war party as Blood Indians and says he gets along with Bloods and Blackfeet from the beaver-trading days; TheBestNotes wrongly frames it as 'Blood, not Utes' (Utes never appear). TheBestNotes has Hugh and Gus riding into town together; in the text Hugh stays behind and says he will follow on foot. Hugh claims seven dead Indians; Gus says five. Miles City is reached in about five hours; Gus sees the town four or five miles east from a slope north of the Yellowstone, so the town is on the river's north side in the novel's geography. Hugh says Miles City has two doctors, both drunkards. Gus tells Hugh he comes from Lonesome Dove on the Rio Grande. Gus introduces himself as a Captain in the Texas Rangers.

- **Storyline:** Hat Creek outfit
- **Where:** Small hot upstairs room in Miles City, Montana Territory, with a window onto the prairie, across the street from a saloon with a piano; Miles City street below the window; Remembered/planned: Clara's orchard, a little grove of live oaks on the south Guadalupe River, Texas; Mentioned: the Musselshell River (where Gus holed up), Nebraska (Clara's), San Antonio (Gus's bank), Tennessee (Gus's youth)

- **When:** Autumn (cool night). Gus proposes the body wait the winter and travel in spring. About two days after Call left the herd.

- **Key events:**
  1. Gus wakes to find his left leg amputated by the drunken Dr. Mobley and refuses at gunpoint to let him take the blackened right leg.
  1. He drinks through the afternoon, tosses a gold piece to Dora, the saloon pianist, and eats venison he cannot keep down.
  1. Call arrives at night, having found the fight site and Pea's gear, and fails to talk Gus into the second amputation.
  1. Gus asks to be buried in Clara's orchard on the Guadalupe in Texas, leaves his half of the herd to Lorena, tells Call he told Newt the truth, and asks for the Hat Creek sign over his grave.
  1. Gus writes notes to Clara and Lorena, asks that Pea get his saddle, and dies at nightfall while Call dozes.

  - *Same room and the street below* | afternoon | unspecified | Augustus McCrae, Dr. Mobley, Jim, Dora: Gus levels his pistol to send the stooping giant Jim away, then flips a twenty-dollar gold piece out the window to the pockmarked girl in black, who goes back in and plays again.
  - *Same room, lantern on the bureau* | night | cool night, a little moonlight | Augustus McCrae, Call, Dr. Mobley: Call looks at the black leg and Gus cocks his pistol on him; they talk of burial in Texas, Lorena, Newt and the sign.
  - *Same room by the window* | sunset into dark | unspecified | Augustus McCrae, Call, Dr. Mobley: Gus gazes out at Montana, scrawls two notes, drifts into a silvery Tennessee-morning mist; later the doctor's lamp shows blood dripping from the sheet.
- **Visual details:** Dr. Mobley: small, fat, red-streaked cheeks, black frock coat sprinkled with dandruff, disreputable old bowler hat on the bureau. · Gus's left leg is gone; the thick stump bandage is leaking blood; the remaining leg is black. · Jim is so tall he must stoop in the doorway and bumps his head leaving. · Dora: skinny, pockmarked, in a black dress; she is said to have studied music in Philadelphia. · Bartender: timid, with a walrus mustache as good as Dish Boggett's; brings venison. · Gus sails a twenty-dollar gold piece into the street. · Gus vomits into a brass spittoon. · Call carries a lantern; one note is left on the bed with a little blood on it, which Call wipes on his pants leg.
- **Notes:** Burial site named explicitly: a little grove of live oaks on the south Guadalupe, called Clara's orchard because Gus and Clara picnicked there; Gus reminds Call they stopped there briefly (the earlier chapter on the way toward Austin). Call identifies the siege creek as the Musselshell River, learned from the old trapper. Call rode the Hell Bitch day and night, recovered Pea Eye's rifle, boots and shirt and Gus's saddle before reaching Miles City. Gus refuses revenge on the Indians. FIRST APPEARANCE: Dr. Mobley, Dora. The Hat Creek sign is confirmed to be riding in the wagon and Gus asks it be placed over his grave. Gus's jug is alluded to (the springhouse). Shmoop is accurate here; GradeSaver omits the sign request.

The blue pigs are present (under the wagon) - they will be eaten at Christmas in ch100. The Texas-burial wager: Lippy bets Call won't do it, Dish takes the bet.
