### Chapter 98

*Opening cue:* “Old High Auld soon replaced…” · *Words:* 4,318 · *Est. start (Horsley):* 34:29 · *Confidence:* high

- **Storyline:** Montana ranch
- **Where:** Missouri River crossing near Fort Benton, Montana Territory (tiny Army outpost); Marais River (crossed; Old Dog lost the next day); Milk River, the last river before Canada, with snow-covered Rockies visible to the west; Ranch headquarters: a timbered creek off the Milk River, a day's drive east of where they first struck it; range between the Milk and the Missouri; Box canyon about fifteen miles from headquarters where wild horses are caught; Fort Benton and Fort Buford (beef delivery trips); Missouri River ice at Fort Benton; Mentioned: Miles City bank, Nebraska, the Yellowstone, south of the Brazos
- **Who (on page):** Captain Woodrow Call, Hugh Auld, Major Court, Jasper Fant, Ben Rainey, Newt Dobbs, Dish Boggett, Needle Nelson, Soupy Jones, Pea Eye Parker, Po Campo, Lippy Jones, Bert Borum, Pete Spettle, the Rainey boys, Saw (Blackfoot chief), Blood Indians at Fort Benton, soldiers, Old Dog (killed), the Texas bull, Sugar, the Hell Bitch, two grizzly bears
- **Mentioned:** Augustus McCrae (dead; appears in Call's dreams), Lorena Wood
- **When:** Autumn into winter: several light snows before the Missouri, a crisp fall day at the Milk, then blizzards; Call's contract is for delivery by Christmas; Newt's month at Fort Benton falls later in the winter.
- **Herd:** Crosses the Missouri near Fort Benton, then the Marais, then reaches the Milk River; driven a day east along the Milk to the headquarters creek. The drive is over; the range lies between the Milk and the Missouri.
- **Key events:**
  1. Old Hugh rejoins with the supply wagon; the outfit crosses the icy Missouri near Fort Benton, where Major Court buys two hundred head and Jasper is nearly drowned when a beaver spooks his horse.
  1. The day after crossing the Marais, two grizzlies are found eating Old Dog, setting off a stampede; a few days later the herd reaches the Milk River.
  1. Call picks a timbered creek for headquarters, ends the drive, and a three-day blizzard hits while the men build a log house, with Po Campo making the fireplace and chimney; Call lives in Wilbarger's tent.
  1. The men capture eighteen wild horses in a box canyon, and Dish, riding Sugar with a spare buckskin, leaves for Nebraska to court Lorena.
  1. Call wins an Army contract for three hundred beeves by Christmas; Newt proves a gifted bronc rider and spends a month breaking horses at Fort Benton, once crashing through the Missouri ice.
- **Scenes (visual beats):**
  - *North bank of the Missouri near Fort Benton* | unspecified | bitterly cold after light snows | crew, Jasper Fant, Ben Rainey, Hugh Auld: The men build a huge bonfire; blue-lipped Jasper sits wrapped in blankets after Ben Rainey pulls him from the icy river.
  - *Trail behind the herd past the Marais* | morning | unspecified | Newt, the Rainey boys, two grizzlies: Riding back for the missing lead steer, the drag riders find two grizzlies feeding on Old Dog; the horses bolt and the whole herd and remuda stampede.
  - *Milk River valley; creek headquarters* | day and night over three days | howling blizzard, no visibility, then a hard crust and deeper cold | crew, Po Campo, Pea Eye, Newt, Dish: The crew huddles between two big fires; Dish's mustache freezes; Po Campo in his serape urges them to shoot a bear.
  - *Headquarters after breakfast* | morning | light snowfall overnight, plains white for miles | Dish, Call, Po Campo, Jasper, Soupy, Pea Eye, Newt: Call writes Dish's wage order on a tablet resting on a frying pan; Dish rides off on Sugar leading a little buckskin until he is a black speck on the snow.
  - *Sapling corral at headquarters; later Fort Benton and the Missouri ice* | unspecified | cold but often fine and sunny | Newt, Call on the Hell Bitch, a hammer-headed bay, a sorrel gelding, soldiers: Newt murmurs to a hammer-headed bay while Call watches silently from the mare; later a runaway sorrel carries him onto the river ice and breaks through in shallow water.
- **Visual details:** Major Court: lanky; Fort Benton is a tiny, poorly provisioned outpost. · The great line of the Rockies is visible to the west; the mountain slopes are snow-covered at the Milk. · Most men wear their new coats at the Milk on a crisp fall day. · The Texas bull is now one-eyed and one-horned with an unevenly sewn wound, and leads the herd; he attacks from his blind side. · Sizable rough log cabin with Po Campo's mud-brick fireplace and chimney; later corrals, a smokehouse and a barn begun. · Call sleeps in Wilbarger's old tent on the creek with a small fire in front. · Po Campo wears a serape and an old scarf; Newt ties a flannel shirt over his ears. · The wild horses are smallish and fat; Bert Borum ropes eighteen. · Dish rides Sugar and leads a little buckskin; he is loaded with provisions and a rifle. · A strong black mustang throws Ben Rainey into a tree and breaks his arm; Newt rides it to a standstill. · Blackfoot chief the soldiers call Saw for his sharp features; Blood Indians also present at the fort. · Fort Benton is full of Indians when beef is delivered; buffalo and elk meat pile up at the ranch.
- **Notes:** Ranch site: a creek with sheltering timber off the Milk River, a day east of where they struck it; Call will file on the land in spring. Old Hugh says north of the Milk is Canada. Old Dog (the lead steer) dies here, eaten by two grizzlies. Dish leaves on Sugar. Call keeps Gus's letters rather than risk them with Dish. Fort Buford is also named as a beef customer. The Blackfoot trade and the Blood Indians seen at Fort Benton: Call suspects some were Gus's killers. GradeSaver says 'an outpost near Fort Benton'; the text calls the outpost Fort Benton itself. The novel's own text misprints the name as 'Old High Auld' in the chapter's opening line. The pigs are not mentioned this chapter.

### Chapter 99

*Opening cue:* “July Johnson Proposed to Clara…” · *Words:* 4,308 · *Est. start (Horsley):* 34:55 · *Confidence:* high

- **Storyline:** Clara
- **Where:** Clara Allen's horse ranch on the Platte River, Nebraska (near Ogallala per earlier chapters; not named here): kitchen, upstairs sickroom, tool shed, horse lots, upper porch; Mentioned: the Platte River (Dish's route), Wyoming, Texas, Montana
- **Who (on page):** July Johnson, Clara Allen, Martin Johnson (July's baby son), Lorena Wood, Betsey Allen, Sally Allen, Cholo, Dish Boggett, soldiers needing horses, Clara's sorrel stallion
- **Mentioned:** Augustus McCrae (dead), Bob Allen (Clara's late husband), Roscoe Brown, Elmira (July's late wife, implied as Martin's mother), the doctor (sent for), Clara's three dead boys
- **When:** Winter: first week of the new year (January) for the proposal; Dish arrives in a January blizzard; the chapter runs through the winter into spring (colts coming).
- **Herd:** n/a - chapter set at Clara's ranch in Nebraska
- **Key events:**
  1. In the first week of the new year July blurts a marriage proposal as he brings frozen potatoes into the kitchen; Clara answers only by asking him to taste her cake batter.
  1. Baby Martin falls dangerously ill; Clara keeps a lone vigil, briefly clings to July, and next morning in the cold scolds him for not sitting with her and tells him to ask again in a year.
  1. Dish Boggett arrives through a January blizzard along the Platte with news of Gus's death; Clara hires him, and July grows jealous of his skill.
  1. Lorena sinks into silent grief, close only to Betsey; Clara tells her Gus loved them both but could never be kept home.
  1. Dish courts Lorena hopelessly all winter, yet stays in spring to help with the colts.
- **Scenes (visual beats):**
  - *Clara's warm kitchen* | unspecified | very cold outside; potatoes frozen | July, Clara, Martin, Lorena, Betsey, Sally: July sets down a sack of frozen potatoes and asks Clara to marry him; she holds out a batter-covered finger for him to taste.
  - *Upstairs sickroom* | evening | unspecified | Clara, July, Martin: Clara rocks the rasping, feverish baby's cradle, then abruptly lays her head on July's chest in tears.
  - *Tool shed and horse lots* | morning | sunny but very cold; breath clouds | Clara, July, sorrel stallion: July is taking a cracked wagon wheel off when Clara storms over, refuses his offered coat, lays into him, then rubs her favourite stallion's neck.
  - *Big kitchen table at night; Clara's icy upper porch* | night | bitter winter cold | Dish, Clara, Betsey, Sally, Lorena, July: Dish plays cards and charades with Clara and the girls while Lorena and July sit silent against the wall; later Clara sits alone on the porch in Bob's huge coat, a cold tear on her cheek.
  - *Upstairs hall and sewing room* | unspecified | unspecified | Clara, Lorena: Clara hugs the uncombed, grief-struck Lorena and later explains why neither woman could have kept Gus home.
- **Visual details:** Martin is a crawling baby who laughs and grabs spoons. · Clara in winter wears her late husband's oversized coat and overshoes, no gloves, large bony hands, and caps, favourite being an old Army cap Cholo found on the plains, sometimes with a wool scarf tied over it; her hair spills over the collar. · Clara has gray eyes. · Clara's favourite horse is a sorrel stallion that whinnies from the lots. · Upper porch crusted with ice; stars dimmed by cold. · Dish arrives with two exhausted horses after following the Platte in a January blizzard. · Lorena, still beautiful but grave, uncombed; Betsey reads stories to her since Lorena can barely read.
- **Notes:** The ranch is not named in this chapter; earlier chapters place it near Ogallala on the Platte. Martin is July's son by Elmira. Clara's 'ask me in a year' is the only answer to the proposal. GradeSaver says Dish 'decides to stay the winter' - in fact Clara offers him a job; at chapter's end he stays past spring for the colts. Clara reveals Gus proposed to her thirty-odd times and looked relieved when she chose Bob. Lorena calls herself a whore aloud for the first time. The sorrel stallion is Clara's favourite (not to be confused with the little sorrel she gave Newt).

### Chapter 100

*Opening cue:* “It Came To Rankle Call…” · *Words:* 5,505 · *Est. start (Horsley):* 35:22 · *Confidence:* high

- **Storyline:** Montana ranch
- **Where:** Hat Creek ranch headquarters on a creek off the Milk River, Montana Territory: log house, corrals, Wilbarger's tent; Nearby Indian camps (Blackfeet among them) visited with Old Hugh; Horsethieves' camp just across the border in Canada, a day north of the line (caught on the third day); Fort Benton (Newt's test cattle sale; source of new hands and horses); Banks of the Milk River where cattle bog, with Canada lying behind Newt to the north; Mentioned: Miles City (bank for Lorena's share; source of two new cowboys), Missouri (Big Tom's forge), Kentucky, Oregon, Mississippi (Call's father's death), Nebraska
- **Who (on page):** Captain Woodrow Call, Newt Dobbs, Pea Eye Parker, Needle Nelson, Hugh Auld, Po Campo, Lippy Jones, Jasper Fant, Soupy Jones, Bert Borum, the Spettle boy (Pete), old horsethief preacher, Big Tom (his son), the Raineys, three Kentucky ex-soldiers, two cowboys from Miles City, Jim (Oregon-bound drifter), the Hell Bitch, Greasy the mule, Jerry the dun, the Texas bull, Gus's pigs (killed)
- **Mentioned:** Augustus McCrae, Lorena Wood, Maggie (Newt's mother), Call's father, Jake Spoon (hanged), Sean O'Brien (died of snakebite), the Irishman (may go home), Clara (gave Newt his sorrel)
- **When:** Winter through spring: pigs killed at Christmas; horse theft in early spring with thin snow; branding with spring grass through thin May snows; Call leaves toward the end of May, the body now about six months dead.
- **Herd:** Ranch established; cattle spread over the range between the Milk and the Missouri; several hundred calves born since Texas are branded.
- **Key events:**
  1. Call resents banking half of every sale for Lorena, lives in Wilbarger's tent all winter, and at Christmas the men kill Gus's pigs; Jasper learns to cook under Po Campo.
  1. In early spring fifteen horses are stolen; Call's party tracks them into Canada, shoots the drunken old preacher who wounds Needle with a hatchet, spares his son Tom, then hangs him ten days later for stealing wallets and Bert's saddle.
  1. Call gives Newt growing responsibility; Soupy objects, trips Newt and beats him in a fight, then leaves with Bert for Texas while new hands drift in.
  1. After a ten-day roundup and a week of branding in May, Call spends afternoons watching Newt break horses but cannot tell him he is his son.
  1. At the end of May Call names Newt range boss, gives him the Hell Bitch, his Henry rifle and his father's gold watch, and rides away south with Greasy, Jerry and the Hat Creek sign.
- **Scenes (visual beats):**
  - *Horsethieves' camp on the prairie in Canada* | morning (breakfast) | thin snow on the ground | Call, Pea Eye, Newt, Needle, Hugh Auld, old preacher, Big Tom: Call walks in with pistol drawn on a drunk graybeard reading a Bible and a huge sweaty boy cooking venison; the old man swings a hatchet at Needle and Call shoots him.
  - *Ranch headquarters before dawn* | dawn | cold | Call, Pea Eye, Big Tom, cowboys in long johns: Call shoots Big Tom off a stolen horse, clubs him with the Henry barrel, and hangs him as the men watch in silence.
  - *Around the supper fire at headquarters* | night | unspecified | Soupy, Newt, Call: Soupy trips Newt carrying his plate; Newt flies at him and is pounded bloody while Call watches impassively.
  - *Corrals at headquarters* | afternoon | sometimes wet or squally | Call, Newt, mustangs: For two weeks Call sits for hours silently watching Newt gentle the new horses.
  - *Muddy banks of the Milk River* | morning | cold and blowy | Call, Newt, Pea Eye, Needle, the Hell Bitch, Greasy, Jerry, the Texas bull: Mud-caked from lifting bogged yearlings, Newt saddles the Hell Bitch with his own saddle as Call, choking on words, gives him the Henry and a thin gold watch and rides off to a speck on the plain.
- **Visual details:** Lippy tries all winter to build a fiddle from a shoebox, producing screeching. · Old preacher: shaky, dirty gray beard, single-shot buffalo gun, a jug by his saddle, muttering over a Bible. · Big Tom: big as an ox, about Newt's age, large always-sweaty face, cap-and-ball pistol; a blacksmith who shoes all the horses. · No decent hanging tree for miles on the plain. · Newt at seventeen has filled out physically; Pea says he walks just like Call. · Greasy is the big mule that came from Texas; Jerry is a rangy dun, Call's second-favourite mount. · Gus's old sign is tied to the pack mule. · Newt had been riding the little sorrel Clara gave him. · Call's Henry fits Newt's scabbard imperfectly, replacing Newt's Winchester. · Call's father's watch: old pocket watch with a thin gold case. · The one-eyed Texas bull bellows and paws with a few cows a hundred yards off.
- **Notes:** FATE OF THE BLUE PIGS: killed and eaten at Christmas. The Hell Bitch passes to Newt, along with Call's Henry rifle and his father's gold watch. FIRST APPEARANCE by name: Jerry the dun (Greasy the mule is older). Call sets out WITHOUT the body; it is still in Mobley's shed at Miles City (ch101). Call tells Newt he'll be gone about a year and to bank Lorena's half in Miles City. Pea mentions Call hanged Jake. GradeSaver says Call makes Newt boss before the Soupy fight; broadly right. TheBestNotes claims Call sends Newt to Fort Benton to break horses again - text says he sends Newt to sell cattle. Shmoop says Tom tried to steal a horse; he also took four wallets and Bert's saddle. Newt says bitterly he is kin to nobody.

### Chapter 101

*Opening cue:* “In Miles City, Call found…” · *Words:* 3,295 · *Est. start (Horsley):* 35:55 · *Confidence:* medium

- **Storyline:** Call's return south
- **Where:** Miles City, Montana Territory: Dr. Mobley's harness shed, the carpenter's; Plains south of Miles City, riding alongside about a hundred Crow Indians; Wyoming: eleven empty days; gullies near the Powder River; Approach to Nebraska; the Platte River, full of ducks and geese; Clara Allen's horse ranch in Nebraska (near Ogallala per earlier chapters): house, lots, back porch, the buggy by the yard, the shack by the lots
- **Who (on page):** Captain Woodrow Call, Dr. Mobley, the carpenter, old Crow chief and his band, five drunk young Indians, Clara Allen, Lorena Wood, Dish Boggett, July Johnson, Betsey Allen, Sally Allen, Martin (toddler), Cholo (coughing, offstage in the shack), Greasy the mule, Jerry the dun, Gus's body in the coffin
- **Mentioned:** Augustus McCrae, Newt Dobbs, the men on the Milk, Clara's dead boys, Kiowa raiders July saw Gus kill (memory)
- **When:** Late spring into early summer, inferred: Call left the Milk at the end of May; the Miles City departure is on an unseasonably cold morning; the Platte teems with ducks and geese. No month named.
- **Herd:** n/a - Call is travelling south with the body; the herd remains on the Milk River ranch.
- **Key events:**
  1. In Miles City Call finds an animal broke into the shed, splintered the coffin and carried off Gus's amputated leg; he has the coffin reinforced and buys an old buggy, tearing off its ragged hood.
  1. He travels south with a friendly Crow band, through empty Wyoming and past the Powder, and faces down five drunk young Indians near Nebraska.
  1. At Clara's ranch Clara berates him for hauling the body and urges burial there; he hands over Gus's two notes, and Clara sobs against the mule.
  1. Lorena stands beside the coffin all night clutching her unread note; Clara reads hers privately, in which Gus asks her to look after Lorena.
  1. Lorena faints next morning; Call hitches up and leaves while Clara strides beside his horse denouncing him for abandoning his unnamed son.
- **Scenes (visual beats):**
  - *Dr. Mobley's shed, Miles City; then the road out* | morning | unseasonably cold, pale sun through frigid clouds | Call, Dr. Mobley, Greasy, Jerry: Call leaves town with a heavy plank-reinforced, canvas-covered coffin lashed to the seat of a hoodless buggy drawn by Greasy.
  - *Plains heading south* | unspecified | improved, fine | Call, Crow chief, Crow band: A dried-up little chief hungry for tobacco and talk rides beside Call among a hundred Crow, all curious about the man in the box.
  - *Clara's yard and lots* | afternoon | unspecified | Call, Clara, Dish, July, Greasy: Bareheaded Clara in overshoes, planting shrubs, turns on Call; after taking the notes she presses her face to the mule's neck and weeps.
  - *By the buggy in the yard; Clara's upper porch* | night, moon rising late | unspecified | Lorena, Clara: Lorena stands like a statue by the coffin all night holding her letter; Clara sits an hour on her porch twisting her hands.
  - *Ranch yard* | morning | unspecified | Call, Clara, Lorena, Dish, July: Lorena crumples and is carried upstairs; Call trots the dun and the mule away, the squeaking buggy bouncing, as Clara strides alongside raging.
- **Visual details:** The coffin is splintered by a wolverine or cougar (doctor's guess), then reinforced with strong planks and covered in canvas. · Old sturdy buggy, hood torn off; Greasy (a brown mule, normally skittish, used to the wagon) pulls it; Call rides the dun, Jerry. · Greasy loses flesh from the pace. · Five drunk Indians, none apparently eighteen, on poor horses; one waves an old pistol. · Call looks aged: little flesh on his face, beard and mustache flecked with gray. · Clara's note is stained at one corner with dried blood. · Betsey and Sally on the back porch with a toddling Martin between them. · Lorena, blonde, stands by the buggy clutching the letter with her name on it.
- **Notes:** Route south: Miles City, riding with Crow, Wyoming (eleven days without a soul), gullies near the Powder River, then Nebraska along the Platte. The amputated leg is lost to an animal. Clara refers to Newt as Call's son outright and vows to write Newt so he gets Call's name. Call says he values the horse more than the name. Clara says she kept Gus in memory for sixteen years. July recalls Gus killing the Kiowa at a campfire. Clara's ranch is unnamed here (Ogallala area per ch76-77). Gus's note to Clara is quoted in the novel; here only paraphrased: he asks her to look after Lorie and wishes her luck with the horses. Shmoop says Call 'bundles Gus up' in Miles City - he actually keeps the reinforced coffin until the Pecos (ch102).

### Chapter 102

*Opening cue:* “So Captain Call Turned back…” · *Words:* 5,265 · *Est. start (Horsley):* 36:15 · *Confidence:* medium

- **Storyline:** Call's return south
- **Where:** South from the Platte across the Republican River, Nebraska, into Kansas (cattle trails full of herds); Colorado: Denver (telegraph office); Purgatoire River (the Picketwire), Colorado: night camp where Goodnight appears; Raton Pass, then the New Mexico plain with snow on the Sangre de Cristo peaks to the north; Santa Rosa, New Mexico: jail and new three-story courthouse with rooftop gallows; Down the Pecos through the Bosque Redondo, New Mexico; Pecos River a day above Horsehead Crossing, Texas: ambush site where the buggy wrecks; Dry country toward the Colorado (and San Saba) rivers of Texas; Clara's orchard: little hill with live oaks above the Guadalupe River, Texas hill country, with a pool below; Around San Antonio (skirted); across the Nueces; Lonesome Dove, Texas: Hat Creek house and roofless barn, dry bed of Hat Creek, main street, ashes of the Dry Bean
- **Who (on page):** Captain Woodrow Call, Denver undertaker, a boy in Denver, Charles Goodnight, Sheriff Owensby, Deputy Decker, two jail deputies, Blue Duck, hangman and crowd at Santa Rosa, red-headed Denver newspaper boy, four Indians (one shot), young settler couple and children, Bolivar, Dillard Brawley, Jerry the dun, Greasy the mule (dies), Gus's body
- **Mentioned:** Clara Allen, Augustus McCrae, Wilbarger and his brother, Deets and Blue Duck (as mirages), Jake Spoon, Newt and the boys on the Milk, the widow Spettle, Sean O'Brien (the Irish boy, snakebite), Xavier Wanz (died burning the Dry Bean), Lorena ('the woman'), Lippy
- **When:** Full summer in Kansas; a dry year on the llano; Call reaches Lonesome Dove late on a day in August, roughly a year after the drive left.
- **Herd:** n/a - Call alone with the body; herd remains at the Milk River ranch in Montana.
- **Key events:**
  1. Stung by Clara's words, Call drives south through Kansas in full summer, then veers into Colorado to avoid questions, sending Wilbarger's brother a telegram in Denver.
  1. On the Purgatoire, Charles Goodnight tells him Blue Duck is caught and will hang at Santa Rosa; Call crosses Raton Pass and waits there while the buggy is rebuilt.
  1. Blue Duck taunts Call from his chains, then on hanging day dives through a third-floor courthouse window dragging Deputy Decker to his death; the sheriff hangs the corpse anyway.
  1. Going down the Pecos in drought, Call is shot in the side a day above Horsehead Crossing; the buggy wrecks, he rewraps Gus in a tarp on a travois made of the sign, and Greasy dies before the Colorado.
  1. Call buries Gus under the live oaks above the Guadalupe with the surviving sign plank as marker, scratches A.M. on its back, then rides to Lonesome Dove in August, finding Bolivar ringing the bell and the Dry Bean burned by Wanz.
- **Scenes (visual beats):**
  - *Denver street outside the telegraph office* | unspecified | unspecified | Call, undertaker, crowd, a boy: A crowd fingers the coffin while a shiny undertaker with waxed mustache and blue bow tie offers a ten-dollar funeral.
  - *Camp on the Purgatoire River* | dusk | unspecified | Call, Charles Goodnight: A big man on a red mule rides up to the fire, declines to dismount, and delivers news of Blue Duck in a few curt lines.
  - *Santa Rosa courthouse square* | unspecified | unspecified | Call, Blue Duck, Decker, Owensby, crowd: Glass shatters as the chained Blue Duck flies from a third-floor window with Decker, smiling at Call as he falls to the stony ground.
  - *Pecos River bank a day above Horsehead Crossing* | afternoon | still, hot, drought | Call, four Indians, Greasy, Jerry: A short brown man rises from behind a yucca; Call, bleeding at the side, careens down the bank and the buggy smashes on a rock, coffin underwater.
  - *Hill with live oaks above the Guadalupe; then Lonesome Dove at sunset/dusk* | day, then sunset into dusk | hot | Call, settler family, Bolivar, Dillard Brawley: Call rests in the half-dug grave, then plants the splintered sign; weeks later he rides into Lonesome Dove to the clang of the dinner bell beaten by a white-haired Bolivar.
- **Visual details:** The Miles City buggy ends up patched so often with wire from trail bosses that it is mostly wire. · Indians along the way include Pawnee, Arapahoe and Ogallala Sioux on horses fat with spring grass. · Goodnight rides a red mule; he has interests in Pueblo. · Santa Rosa courthouse: three stories, gallows at the top, fine glass windows, polished floors; town crowded with cowhands and families sleeping in wagons. · Blue Duck: huge head, snake-cold eyes, larger than Call expected, greasy rag bound round a head wound, shot in shoulder and leg, handcuffed and wrapped in chains bolted to the wall. · Deputy Decker is fat and drunk; Sheriff Owensby is balding and too nervous to eat. · Newspaper boy: red-headed, scarcely twenty, scribbling on a pad. · Llano brown with drought, mirages shimmering, dry lightning at night; the leaking coffin trails fine salt. · Travois: the sign lashed with rope between the two buggy wheels on a salt-cedar axle, Gus wrapped in Call's tarp. · The sign: Latin motto at the bottom broken off, pig and rental lines, Deets's, Pea's and Call's names gone; only the top line naming the Hat Creek Cattle Company and Livery Emporium survives, tied as a crossbar to a mesquite stick; Call scratches A.M. on the back. · Settlers: sunburned beet-red fair young man, wife in a close bonnet, two or three narrow-faced children. · Bolivar: white hair, filthier serape, beating the bell with the broken crowbar; the house is full of cobwebs and rat droppings; the barn is roofless; the stone trough holds clear water. · Dillard Brawley: one-legged barber with a ruined whisper of a voice, carrying a few perch.
- **Notes:** WHERE CALL IS SHOT: the text says his route ran down the Pecos past Bosque Redondo and on south through New Mexico, then was shot a day above Horsehead Crossing (Horsehead Crossing is in Texas), so the ambush is on the Pecos just north of Horsehead Crossing, likely in Texas; GradeSaver's 'while traveling through New Mexico' is imprecise. The attackers are four unnamed Indians; Call kills one. DENVER: GradeSaver says he rode through Denver to avoid crowds; in the text he turned west into Colorado to avoid the cattle trails, and Denver itself drew a crowd he regretted. GradeSaver/Shmoop say he carried Gus on the mule; the text has a travois made from the sign on the buggy wheels, pulled by Greasy and then by Jerry after Greasy dies. Shmoop says Lonesome Dove is 'nearby' the grave; in fact Call rides south past San Antonio and across the Nueces. Shmoop calls Dillard Brawley 'a cowboy'. Call expected a doctor in Austin but actually skirts San Antonio. The orchard is described only as a hill with live oaks above the Guadalupe (ch96 said the south Guadalupe); the settlers head down the ridge toward San Antonio, which supports placing it in the hill country north of San Antonio. Call says it is five hundred miles from the wreck to the south Guadalupe. Blue Duck's death is here (Santa Rosa, New Mexico). FINAL APPEARANCE: the dinner bell and broken crowbar; the Hat Creek sign (only the company-name line remains; Latin motto lost). Wanz burned the Dry Bean, dying in Lorena's old room with the piano. Ignore trailing table-of-contents junk.
